**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-16 03:21 UTC

Need review/review tags for PRs

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-16 03:22 UTC

Interesting; how do those work?

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-16 03:22 UTC

Pretty much PRs are labeled with "needs review" initially. A zulip maintainer will then review the PR and the label will be switched to "reviewed"

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-16 03:23 UTC

If a person is inactive on the "reviewed" PR for a whole week, then he/she gets notified that someone has already looked at the PR

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-16 03:23 UTC

If a PR hasn't gotten a review on a "needs review" PR, I think the area team gets mentioned to review the PR.

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-16 03:26 UTC

Wait, it isn't the area team actually, it's the pull request assignees (reviewers).

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-16 05:44 UTC

In general, best practices for a Git repository is that once you push a mistake into master, and folks might have pulled those commits and started doing things with them, the recommended best practice is to fix forward by pushing a bug fix commit, rather than force-pushing.  My rule for zulip.git is at most 5 minutes must have passed and the mistake needs to be significant; something more relaxed is probably fine for zulipbot.git though :)

So my recommendation would be to move those into a new `secrets.js` file

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-18 04:51 UTC

at least seconds showing the math, e.g. 3600 * 24 * 7 or whatever

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-18 04:53 UTC

And then I'd recommend just checking that file in with some name like `zulip_project_config.js` and then in our deploy we can have a symlink from `config.js` to that, so that Zulip's config is managed via the repo.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-06-16 05:46 UTC

It's the encrypted and compressed package of `src/secrets.js` and `src/config.js`; since they're included in the `.gitignore`, those files aren't included in GitHub, but they need to be provided to Travis for builds to pass

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-06-16 08:21 UTC

**Yago González** · 2017-06-16 08:27 UTC

I believe it's with the Travis' CLI tool.

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-06-16 08:27 UTC

**Yago González** · 2017-06-16 08:31 UTC

There are times when you need API secrets and such in your tests, but you can't reveal them in the source or the logs. That's why Travis allows to encrypt some environment variables and files.

**Yago González** · 2017-06-24 12:46 UTC

**Yago González** · 2017-06-24 20:04 UTC

**Yago González** · 2017-06-24 20:05 UTC

**Yago González** · 2017-06-24 20:06 UTC

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-06-24 20:06 UTC

**Yago González** · 2017-06-24 20:10 UTC

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-06-24 20:10 UTC

## auto-watching repositories

*2017-02-28 to 2017-02-28 · 3 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/auto-watching.20repositories/with/158961)*

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-02-28 19:37 UTC

I got the following reply from GitHub to my email about watching repositories and the lack of an API to control that when adding a collaborator:
> This is only true for users who have automatic watching enabled: https://help.github.com/articles/watching-repositories/ Here at GitHub, new employees are warned to disable this feature before they are added to our organization, as otherwise they would get far more than 100 emails per day between al …
I guess we could add a warning in the zulipbot docs that one may want to turn that feature off?  Though I think the default is actually good for some situations...

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-02-28 19:44 UTC

It's already noted in the new contributor message :)

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-02-28 21:59 UTC

yeah, I think their suggestion would be to also put it in the zulipbot docs, but I think that's fairly marginal


## area label teams: new PRs to issues

*2017-03-01 to 2017-03-05 · 70 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/area.20label.20teams.3A.20new.20PRs.20to.20issues/with/159091)*

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-01 00:05 UTC

Currently, our goal is to implement mentioning of area label teams whenever an issue is mentioned in a PR body/comment. However, GitHub supports labeling of PRs; should we implement mentioning of area label teams when the PR is labeled with the corresponding area label instead?

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-01 00:05 UTC

Labeling PRs with a "work in-progress" label might be useful too

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-01 00:14 UTC

This would be pretty useful.

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-01 00:28 UTC

My theory is that we don't really want to do the work of adding area labels to PRs

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-01 00:29 UTC

Given that they should usually mention an issue that we've already done the work of labeling

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-01 00:29 UTC

But I suppose we could shift the design to have zulipbot add labels from issues referenced in a PR automatically to the PR

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-01 00:29 UTC

So that one could add PR labels manually as well

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-01 00:31 UTC

Hmm, this seems to be appropriate. A lot of labeling ahaha

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-01 02:06 UTC

Hm, I think some things might go wrong with automatic PR labeling (some people mention issues/PRs only for reference and not "PR fixes this issue), but yeah I can implement labeling of a PR according to what issue it mentions. Working on it now.

Yes, the `pull_request` event is still sent out but the action is `edited` and you'll have to read from the body differently [source](https://developer.github.com/v3/activity/events/types/#pullrequestevent)

## pending review label

*2017-03-01 to 2017-03-01 · 11 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/pending.20review.20label/with/159151)*

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-01 03:45 UTC

Once we get zulipbot to respond to PRs that reference and issue, we should add a `pending review` label to that issue. Any thoughts?

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-01 03:49 UTC

I think that's an interesting workflow element; to make that work, we'll want to have something that removes that label automatically as well when review comments are posted.

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-01 03:49 UTC

I'm not 100% confident that the review experience is such that we can automatically manage things

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-01 03:49 UTC

E.g. does someone posting a single comment count as a review?

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-01 03:50 UTC

No, those count as issue comments

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-01 03:50 UTC

@Cynthia Lin Single comments can count as reviews.

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-01 03:50 UTC

This does bring up a good point..

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-01 03:51 UTC

I think we should also start using the "approve" and "request changes" review features

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-01 03:51 UTC

If the comment isn't on a file diff, it counts as an "issue_comment" in the payloads

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-01 03:53 UTC

Yea, but if the single comment is on a file diff, then it'll count as a review. I think that "approve" and "request changes" better encompasses this and makes a clear distinction between needing changes and just making some minor comments.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-01 03:54 UTC

yes, that's what I meant. And yeah, I don't see that feature utilized often and I think it would really help contributors who are trying to get PRs approved

## mentioning multiple teams

*2017-03-03 to 2017-03-03 · 26 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/mentioning.20multiple.20teams/with/161215)*

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-03 21:35 UTC

How easy would it be to avoid posting multiple notifications when 2 teams' labels were added?

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-03 22:07 UTC

Pretty easy, we can just join the teams array into a string just like we do with the added issue labels :)

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-03 22:08 UTC

Cool, yeah my only worry was if the GitHub API gave you like 1 hit per label

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-03 23:09 UTC

@Tim Abbott Just did a review of the GitHub API docs, and it looks like that when issues are manually labeled one by one through the GitHub UI, only one issue label payload is sent at a time (this goes with the "labeled" IssuesEvent) , so they'd have to post multiple comments with one label each; however, I can still fix it so that if you manually label an issue with the `@zulipbot label` command, zulipbot can post multiple labels in one comment. Does that sound OK with you?

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-03 23:10 UTC

Well usually in the GitHub UI, I click the 3 labels, and then click out of the label picker, and they all appear after clicking out

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-03 23:10 UTC

So I think that might label multiple at once?

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-03 23:16 UTC

Unfortunately, only one label is sent per `labeled` IssueEvent :\

**Yago González** · 2017-03-03 23:16 UTC

Maybe we could wait a minute, and if no additional tags have been added, then send the message

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-03 23:17 UTC

We should probably encourage users to label through zulipbot instead of the GitHub UI then :thinking_face:

**Yago González** · 2017-03-03 23:17 UTC

Or it'd be cool if we could edit the messages, the same way you can manually edit them with the pencil icon

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-03 23:17 UTC

What message is being edited?

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-03 23:18 UTC

Thing is, I'm not sure if when you edit a comment on GitHub  the new mention will be pinged by the mention

**Yago González** · 2017-03-03 23:18 UTC

I mean, if you add two labels: post the comment mentioning the first label's team, and when getting the second `labeled` IssueEvent, simply edit the comment, adding the new label

**Yago González** · 2017-03-03 23:19 UTC

Hmmm we can try it. Can you post a comment in a test repo, and then edit it mentioning me?

**Yago González** · 2017-03-03 23:20 UTC

(although before this we should make sure that the GitHub API provides the tools to edit comments)

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-03 23:21 UTC

Just mentioned you in an edited comment, check your notifications

**Yago González** · 2017-03-03 23:21 UTC

Got the email

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-03 23:22 UTC

That's good news, we'd have for refactor the code to post only one initial comment but that'd be a good solution, does that sound to you Tim?

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-03 23:23 UTC

The edit game seems probably better than having 2 posts?

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-03 23:24 UTC

Honestly the editing would be very complicated because you'd have to keep track of the initial issue comment ID for each issue, so it might not be that optimal in the end

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-03 23:27 UTC

So basically we can either:
* discourage manual labeling through the GitHub UI and ask others to use zulipbot to label instead, because it can keep track of the multiple issue labels added and concatenate them into one comment
* Post one initial issue comment, somehow keep track of the issue comment ID for each issue for future editing...?
* Just keep it the way it is right now (`labeled` IssuesEvent)

**Yago González** · 2017-03-03 23:27 UTC

Can't we read all the comments in a PR? If so, we could find which one contains the label stuff (with a regex or similar)

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-03 23:28 UTC

Oh I've already implemented issue referencing in PRs (only the first issue mentioned in the PR is tracked though), the issue is when an issue is labeled

**Yago González** · 2017-03-03 23:28 UTC

The flow would be something like: `labeled` event -> get comments in PR -> is any of those a labels message? -> YES: edit it. NO: create a new one

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-03 23:30 UTC

Yeah, that sounds reasonable, thanks for the feedback

**Yago González** · 2017-03-03 23:34 UTC

Happy to help! Let me know if I can give you a hand with anything :)


## advertising

*2017-03-04 to 2017-03-09 · 17 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/advertising/with/162110)*

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-04 21:17 UTC

I wonder if we should provide some sort of clarity for when you should claim an issue

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-04 21:18 UTC

E.g. should you claim when you see an issue that you want to work on (even if that's an intent a few days in the future), or when you work on it, or when you've done a bit of research to figure out how you're going to actually do it, or when you have spent >=1hr coding on it, or something else?  

Potential problems to worry about include that maybe without guidance, someone might claim 15 issues they intend to do over their GSoC summer, and then not get to some of them for months, which is probably not ideal.

**Yago González** · 2017-03-04 21:20 UTC

I guess that we shouldn't really care when the issue is claimed, as long as the job gets done on time. However, I'd establish a maximum amount of issues you can have assigned at the same time

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-04 21:21 UTC

Well, the cost of claiming a lot of issues early is that other contributors won't work on those issues

**Yago González** · 2017-03-04 21:23 UTC

But if you claim it, you know you have to get it done by X day. I think it's like the tasks approach in the GCI

**Yago González** · 2017-03-04 21:27 UTC

I guess what is really important is to set a reasonable timeout for claimed issues, that grants you some margin provided that you don't forget about it for a week

**Yago González** · 2017-03-04 21:28 UTC

It could be interesting to keep an eye on the average time it takes for a claimed issue to get closed

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-04 21:29 UTC

I think this is a time where the inactive claimed issue feature we're trying to implement comes into effect

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-04 21:30 UTC

Going through the repository so far, it looks like the issues claimed range from actively working on an issue to "I have an interest on working on this issue and I know how to resolve it, but I have yet to start working on it"

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-04 21:31 UTC

I think implementing a claimed issue limit could potentially solve this problem since it's really hard to multitask on multiple issues

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-04 21:35 UTC

Personally I believe that issues should be claimed only if A) You have an interest in working on it and B) You have a vague of idea of how you will resolve the issue. Claiming the issue after you've started implementing changes and are ready to open a PR for it doesn't really correlate of the issue being "in progress"

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-04 21:39 UTC

I guess I like the idea of focusing on the outcome rather than the exact state

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-04 21:40 UTC

Because a big project you're going to spend a month on is different from a small project you're going to spend an hour on

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-04 21:42 UTC

Since you're more concerned that the issue actually gets worked on when it's claimed, does implementing the inactive issue feature and an assigned issue limit seem reasonable?

**Yago González** · 2017-03-04 21:43 UTC

Yeah, that's something else we should give a thought: how should big projects work? And, will it be possible to immediately re-claim an issue?

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-04 21:52 UTC

I think we shouldn't worry about bad actors, so yeah, you should be able to immediately re-claim

**Sumana Harihareswara** · 2017-03-09 23:08 UTC

(by the way, one idiom for the problem of claiming a ticket and not working on it for a while, and blocking other people from working on it, is "cookie-licking" -- imagine a platter of cookies. I lick one and put it back. Now I may or may not ever eat it, but no one else can eat it. I mention this in case it is a useful tool for you all when describing the problem and the reason for this feature.)

## travis PR updates

*2017-03-05 to 2017-03-08 · 23 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/travis.20PR.20updates/with/162645)*

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-05 06:13 UTC

Alright, so currently [PR #45](https://github.com/zulip/zulipbot/pull/45) is open to reflect more current progress on posting comments whenever the Travis build for a PR finishes. As you can see, it currently posts the status update without any restraints. 

We discussed the possibility of enabling/disabling this feature through a command, do we want the user to post `@zulipbot travis` every time the PR opener wants an update, or can we just have the PR opener do something like `@zulipbot label travis-updates` to have the PR have a "travis updates" label and just have zulipbot check if the issue has the "travis updates" label and post the update or something else?

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-05 06:14 UTC

## average wait time

*2017-03-19 to 2017-04-06 · 16 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/average.20wait.20time/with/174544)*

**Sumana Harihareswara** · 2017-03-19 20:32 UTC

I just realized it would be nice if I could ask zulipbot:

How long does a Zulip developer usually have to wait to get a review, after submitting a PR?

How much time should a Zulip developer allot for (in total) waiting for reviews, revising work in response to reviews, and reading and writing stuff in pull request comments, etc. -- that is, what is the median amount of time it takes to go from opening to closing PRs?

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-19 20:37 UTC

That's probably an independent thing we could scan for more than something you need to do with an online tool like zulipbot

**Sumana Harihareswara** · 2017-03-19 20:39 UTC

Hmmm, I was thinking that, for instance, when someone mentions a PR in a new topic in `code review`, zulipbot could give an expected wait time :)

**Sumana Harihareswara** · 2017-03-19 20:39 UTC

but yeah, I see what you mean, probably the wrong home for it

**Yago González** · 2017-03-19 21:03 UTC

Someone during the creation of zulipbot suggested adding some kind of ML algorithm to give it some "intelligence". I think this goes along that path, and I think it's *very* cool. Maybe we could even find patterns that would relate characteristics in the PR (like additions/deletions number, area...) with the averate ETAs. I get excited simply by thinking about it, hahaha.

**Yago González** · 2017-03-19 21:03 UTC

However, I think that for the time being we should focus in having a polished workflow with zulipbot, and then go with stuff like this (or even creating a separate, smarter tool that helps us manage PRs).

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-19 21:52 UTC

A lot of people have talked about making zulipbot more interactive by connecting it to Zulip; I think someone suggested PM'ing zulipbot on Zulip for PR reports (Steve if I recall correctly?). Right now my priority is getting the zulipbot GitHub wiki set up properly/writing the blog post/writing unit tests/getting our first release out before your presentation, but I've been keeping the possibility of a Zulip zulipbot being created so it's more of a "how much do you guys want to see a zulipbot Zulip integration" question :)

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-19 21:54 UTC

Yep, Steve made this suggestion. I thought making zulipbot accessible from Zulip chat sounds great.

**Sumana Harihareswara** · 2017-04-06 17:19 UTC

@Joshua Pan @Cynthia Lin when you say "getting our first release out" -- is that something you'll announce on zulip-dev? has it already happened and I missed it? :)

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-04-06 17:20 UTC

No, there hasn't been a release for that yet.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-04-06 17:20 UTC

But yeah we'll definitely announce it on zulip-dev once we're ready :)

**Sumana Harihareswara** · 2017-04-06 17:20 UTC

Got it, thanks. :)

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-04-06 17:21 UTC

For now, I'd like to stabilize any efficiency issues with the PR review system and the inactivity checks before we release

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-04-06 17:22 UTC

@Tim Abbott I haven't seen zulipbot perform an inactivity check for a while now, is it because that feature's been temporarily disabled or is there something going wrong?

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-04-06 17:23 UTC

I think it's because it's disabled

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-04-06 17:25 UTC

Checking the configuration now, everything seems valid; have there been any recent console errors?

## comment reduction

*2017-03-25 to 2017-03-26 · 38 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/comment.20reduction/with/178899)*

**Rishi Gupta** · 2017-03-25 18:34 UTC

Is there a way for zulipbot to email folks directly for the "Hello [..] members, this issue was labeled with the ..", rather than posting to the issue? Is there a way to get people's email via the github api? This might reduce the number of zulipbot comments needed for each thread

Would it be fine to just unassign people at the 10 day mark, without posting a comment? It should still show up as "zulipbot removed the in-progress label", right?

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-25 18:37 UTC

The problem with unassigning people without telling them is that they will never know unless they check. The comment sends them an email.

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-25 18:38 UTC

I don't know if there's a way to send people an email via github api

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-25 18:38 UTC

But, I think we should just edit one comment and add all labeling mentions onto that.

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-25 18:41 UTC

I would think the best solution for the zulipbot labelling mention is the following.

The main labeling comment would look like the following:
```
[LABELING]
@zulip/[team1] look at this, [label1]
@zulip/[team2] look at this, [label2]
```

This takes advantage of mentioning in edits.

**Rishi Gupta** · 2017-03-25 18:42 UTC

Just to clarify, I'm advocating posting a comment at 7 days, but not 10 days. I do think there is probably some benefit in them getting 2 emails instead of one, though, so the ideal situation in my mind is a post on day 7 and just them getting the email on day 10 (along with some non-email indication on the issue thread that they have been unassigned). 

And cool, editing the comment is a great idea, that probably solves all these issues

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-25 18:43 UTC

We can just edit the 7 day notice to the 10 day notice. We can also mention a person and delete the comment right after.

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-25 18:44 UTC

That sends an email to the person/team and the comment is deleted, so it was like it was never there.

**Rishi Gupta** · 2017-03-25 20:32 UTC

Cool. I'm in favor of editing the 7 day notice with an at-mention to the user; no need to email the team again.

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-25 21:09 UTC

Also, right now there's way too many zulipbot inactivity emails happening; we should consider turning off that feature if we can't debug it today

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-25 21:12 UTC

@Tim Abbott can you deploy? I just turned it off.

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-25 21:18 UTC

done

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-26 00:27 UTC

It wasn't deployed properly (Travis builds failed), fixing rn

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-26 00:33 UTC

Yes, it is possible to send emails to members of a team, but it can only send an email if the user has opted to display their email publicly on their GitHub profile.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-26 00:35 UTC

So are we looking to:
* Combine all "this issue was labeled with xxx label @area-label-team" comments into one edited one?
* Edit the inactivity warning comment with the abandon warning comment?

Just for clarification

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-26 00:35 UTC

I like the idea of doing the inactive -> abandon as an edit

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-26 00:36 UTC

I'd also be on board with the issue-labeled one being an edit.

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-26 00:36 UTC

Ideally we want to minimize unnecessary notifications wherever possible

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-26 01:20 UTC

Turns out we already *have* support for issue-label edits; but since most of the time the "labeled" payloads are sent simultaneously, zulipbot can't check whether there was already a previous comment properly

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-26 01:21 UTC

If you were to label an issue with an area label, pause and wait for zulipbot to post the comment, and add another label, then the previous comment is modified properly

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-26 01:22 UTC

But if you add 2 labels at the same time, zulipbot can't find the previous comment because both labels are being added near simultaneously so the comment hasn't been made yet when the second label is being processed

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-03-26 01:26 UTC

Perhaps zulipbot could store its recent actions in memory, and clear those 15 seconds after posting?

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-26 01:36 UTC

Or we could have a designated comment which starts off with `[LABELS]` as the first line.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-26 01:37 UTC

So you mean create a comment when the issue is initially opened?

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-03-26 01:37 UTC

A problem I foresee with that is if people label their issues before posting them, the same thing's bound to happen

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-26 01:38 UTC

No

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-26 01:38 UTC

zulipbot checks if there's any labeling comments

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-26 01:38 UTC

## message conditional on tests passing

*2017-03-30 to 2017-03-30 · 21 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/message.20conditional.20on.20tests.20passing/with/181217)*

**Rishi Gupta** · 2017-03-30 00:07 UTC

Often I want to do the following thing: open a PR for a change, conditioned on Travis passing. Currently I have two options:
* Open the PR and hope that Travis passes
* Push locally, write down in a todo list "check in on the PR later", and open if it passes

The first is bad for reviewers -- it causes PRs, emails, etc for code that is not ready to be reviewed.
The second is bad for contributors, since it adds a lot of cognitive overhead to each PR.

It would be great to be able to open a PR in a special zulipbot repo (or maybe register everyone's fork with zulipbot?), and have some command like `@zulipbot publish` that would create the PR in `zulip/zulip` if/once the tests pass.

A similar request is: often after editing a PR, I want to trigger a "ready for review" email, conditioned on tests passing. In that case I would like to be able to edit (let's say) the first comment that opened the PR with `@zulipbot request-review`, and then zulipbot could post a comment triggering mail once the tests pass.

**Steve Howell** · 2017-03-30 00:20 UTC

When I do reviews, I visually scan for the green checkmarks to see if Travis passes.  I strongly encourage folks to create wip PRs, even though the problems Rishi mentions are nuisances.  The reviewers can deal with a bit of noise.  Putting "wip" in the title is helpful when you know it's truly work in progress (as opposed to just pending-Travis).

**Steve Howell** · 2017-03-30 00:21 UTC

I think it would be worthwhile for reviewers and zulipbot authors to carefully study this page: https://help.github.com/articles/searching-issues/

**Steve Howell** · 2017-03-30 00:23 UTC

For example, here's a query of long-running PRs that might need some extra attention to get them over the finish line:

https://github.com/zulip/zulip/pulls?utf8=%E2%9C%93&q=is%3Apr%20is%3Aopen%20comment%3A%3E10

**Steve Howell** · 2017-03-30 01:34 UTC

And here's a query of PRs that have passed the build:

https://github.com/zulip/zulip/pulls?utf8=%E2%9C%93&q=is%3Apr%20is%3Aopen%20status%3Asuccess

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-03-30 01:35 UTC

oh cool! What would it do? Would it send an email to the author, or send an email to everyone?

    github.issues.getLabels({
      owner: repoOwner,

fs.readdir("./payloads", (err, files) => {

             res.render("index");

       });

});

If the hashed commit isn't the one that conflicts, I'm not sure if it's relevant mentioning it at all. What do you think?

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-06-03 18:24 UTC

Yeah, maybe we should suggest just using `git fetch; git log -p upstream/master` to find what conflicts.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-06-03 18:35 UTC

`git fetch; git log -p upstream/master` shows the latest commit on `master` from what I can tell, and I think we already do that

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-06-03 18:36 UTC

OK we should probably just make it a link to a little doc section

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-06-03 18:36 UTC

If you have a file `foo/bar.py` that conflicts, `git fetch; git log -p upstream/master -- foo/bar.py` will show you changes to that file

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-06-03 18:38 UTC

We already link to the Zulip ReadTheDocs; the git command you pasted above shows the latest commit to the changed file, which may or may not contain the conflicting change

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-06-03 18:39 UTC

Maybe I'll just get rid of the (latest: commit hash) part? We already encourage users to review recent commits so

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-06-03 18:39 UTC

Yea, I think that'll work

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-06-03 18:42 UTC

The command shows all the commits to the changed file

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-06-03 18:42 UTC

Starting with the latest

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-06-03 18:43 UTC

Anyway, I guess what I was thinking is we could add a brief note in the Zulip Git guide about how to do this and do a brief link to that.

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-06-03 18:43 UTC

So that folks who don't yet know `git log -p <filename>` don't end up doing something much more inefficient to try to see what else changed

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-11-22 04:28 UTC

Seems like zulipbot is pulling up old PRs that have merge conflicts because those old warnings did not have the `<-- mergeconflictwarning -->` thing

**Robert Hönig** · 2017-06-01 16:58 UTC

For the PR #5136, I posted the comment `@zulipbot label "area: bots"`, yet zulipbot did not reply. Is zulipbot up, or does it require me to be the creator of the PR (which I am not) for adding labels?

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-06-01 16:59 UTC

You need to be the creator of the PR, yes

**Robert Hönig** · 2017-06-01 16:59 UTC

Aah, okay. Thanks.

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-06-01 17:00 UTC

I wonder if we should relax that requirement

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-06-01 17:01 UTC

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-06-01 17:01 UTC

We already have an option to disable that requirement in the config :)

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-06-01 17:01 UTC

ok cool, maybe we should try toggling it?

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-06-01 17:01 UTC

we also have an issue open for allowing a group of specified users too

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-06-01 17:01 UTC

If you'd be more comfortable with that? https://github.com/zulip/zulipbot/issues/103

**Robert Hönig** · 2017-06-01 17:02 UTC

**Robert Hönig** · 2017-06-01 17:03 UTC

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-06-01 17:03 UTC

It hasn't been implemented yet, so we can do whatever works best :)

**Robert Hönig** · 2017-06-01 17:04 UTC

I think specifying it to an organization would be very useful for many projects

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-06-01 17:18 UTC

members of the organization isn't crazy.  I think if someone doesn't have permission, zulipbot should probably send them an error message though, unless we're letting everyone do it

~~~diff
-                var valid = /https?:\/\/(www\.)?[-a-zA-Z0-9@:%._\+~#=]{2,256}\.[a-z]{2,6}\b([-a-zA-Z0-9@:%_\+.~#?&//=]*)/.test(value);
+                var valid = /https?:\/\/(www\.)?[-a-zA-Z0-9@:%._+~#=]{2,256}\.[a-z]{2,6}\b([-a-zA-Z0-9@:%_+.~#?&//=]*)/.test(value);
~~~

> I'm not sure if I understand removing `\`?

If you notice, it's a character set (enclosed in square braces). In a set the `+` character doesn't have any special meaning, and therefore it isn't necessary to escape it.

**Yago González** · 2017-06-25 00:18 UTC

> Are these added spaces?

I don't really understand the question here. Mind explaining a bit?

**Yago González** · 2017-06-25 00:22 UTC

~~~diff
-  return Git.Repository.open(path.normalize(__dirname + '/..'));
 +  return Git.Repository.open(path.resolve(__dirname, "../"));
~~~

`path.normalize` basically turns a path absoute, taking into account modifiers like `./`, `/..`, etc.

**Yago González** · 2017-06-25 00:23 UTC

The linter apparently doesn't like concatenating strings for paths (which kinda makes sense), so I found out about `path.resolve`, that basically concatenates, "understands" the `..` notation like `normalize`, and returns the absolute path.

**Yago González** · 2017-06-25 00:24 UTC

2 in 1. [These](https://nodejs.org/api/path.html#path_path_resolve_paths) are the docs. I tested it manually, but feel free to give it a go with `console.log` :)

**Yago González** · 2017-06-23 09:29 UTC

For the record, I found out what was going on with the links.

I used `fs.link` because `fs.symlink` was creating broken links for me, but I didn't notice that `.link` creates *hard links* instead of *symlinks*. Extra commit pushed to [zulip/zulipbot#109](https://github.com/zulip/zulipbot/pull/109), and mistery solved ;)

## functionality

*2017-06-25 to 2017-07-26 · 22 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/functionality/with/237049)*

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-06-25 18:25 UTC

As I'm currently refactoring the codebase, I'm thinking about removing the following capabilities:
* Checking for issue references in PR comments (a lot of people mention issues in PRs not as "this PR fixes issue XXX, but a a reference to a different issue)
* removing the `join` command. We personally have disabled it in our config, and I doubt other orgs would find it useful because it hands organization admin permissions to the bot (the reason we ended up disabling it in the first place)

**Yago González** · 2017-06-25 18:29 UTC

Yeah, I agree with both. Seems pretty reasonable.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-06-26 22:24 UTC

Should assignees be removed from issues once they're closed? Sometimes closed issues get reopened because more improvements need to be made, but someone else other than the original issue assignee might intend to work on it.

**Yago González** · 2017-06-26 22:27 UTC

Maybe we could remove the asignees if the issue is reopened, but send a message mentioning them and asking if they're interested in re-taking the issue.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-06-26 22:32 UTC

I like the remove assignees on issue reopen idea, but I think mentioning the old assignee again is a bit unnecessary, since they would've already gotten a notification that the issue was reopened

**Yago González** · 2017-06-26 22:42 UTC

Fair point!

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-06-27 20:47 UTC

The only problem I see with removing assignees when closing an issue is that sometimes I accidentally close an issue by clicking the wrong button and then immediately reopen

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-06-27 21:18 UTC

Well, issues do already have the "in progress" label removed once they're closed; I'm considering moving the assignee removal to an issue's closing instead of on its reopening, since they're kinda related

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-06-27 21:56 UTC

We might want to put time delays on our commands

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-06-28 00:13 UTC

yeah, a time delay would work well for the unassign step.  I wouldn't do time delays in general.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-07-12 18:16 UTC

When contributors are unassigned from inactive issues, I'm thinking that zulipbot should check if the contributor has referenced any pull requests to the issue, and if an inactive issue has a referenced PR, zulipbot should post a comment on the inactive referenced PR that the PR author (inactive issue assignee) has been unassigned from the issue and ask a project maintainer to either close the PR or label the PR as inactive. Thoughts?

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-07-12 18:17 UTC

Hmm, I don't know about closing the PR because we do leave a lot of PRs open that have code

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-07-12 18:18 UTC

That's why zulipbot doesn't automatically close it, but it asks Tim (or maybe posts a report to zulip?) to consider closing the issue or marking it with the "inactive" or "help wanted" labels if he'd like someone to continue working on the pull request with the previous progress made

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-07-12 18:56 UTC

Hmm.  Do you have examples of cases where this tool would have helped?

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-07-12 22:12 UTC

For example, someone was unassigned from #3882 after the issue went inactive, but the pull request #4279 that they opened for it is still open and has no denotations that the PR author was unassigned from the issue and that the PR has gone inactive.

In this case zulipbot would post a report on Zulip/comment on the PR notifying others that the pull request author is no longer working on  the issue and this PR has thus gone inactive, prompting you to either mark it has inactive/help wanted or close the issue

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-07-12 22:33 UTC

I see.  I would consider doing something less aggressive like labeling the PR as "inactive" or something until the author posts again, or something.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-07-12 22:58 UTC

So you'd prefer an default action of zulipbot automatically labeling as "inactive", notifying you that the PR is inactive so you can decide if you want to close it/label it as "help wanted"/etc.?

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-07-12 23:14 UTC

Well, I'm more thinking that we may want to leave the PR open but tagged for someone to pick up over notifying someone.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-07-26 23:21 UTC

GitHub just added (beta) [support](https://developer.github.com/changes/2017-07-26-team-review-request-thor-preview/) for making review requests to organization teams with the API; wondering if we should change posting comments on a PR mentioning area label teams to requesting a review from area label teams instead

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-07-26 23:42 UTC

I'm not sure.  Maybe worth trying making a review request to a team manually?

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-07-26 23:44 UTC

Basically once it sends a review request to the team, anyone on the team can answer the request to approve/request more changes

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-07-26 23:45 UTC

I guess basically all we want is for it to email the team, so I think replacing the comment with a review request for the team could be great.

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-08-02 19:19 UTC

We should probably brainstorm a bit about how to make the Zulipbot workflow to be less error-prone, since potentially buggy notices is getting to be a big problem.

This is an area where if we build the right tooling and APIs (e.g. with a dry-run mode that we always use when testing and is implemented in a way where it'd be hard to accidentally call things directly, that just prints what it would do), we can make it really easy to do development without any risk of accidental posts

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-08-02 19:20 UTC

That same dry-run mode would also be super useful for pre-running operations that might do 100 notifications; one can print them out, hand-check a random sample of 10, and then be pretty confident they're correct and useful.

## merge conflict errors

*2017-08-23 to 2017-08-23 · 10 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/merge.20conflict.20errors/with/271924)*

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-08-23 17:12 UTC

Did it post merge conflict errors on a bunch of random PRs?  I got an email for one on https://github.com/zulip/zulip/pull/6238 (seems to have been deleted?)

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-08-23 17:13 UTC

Those were probably caused by payload delivery delays

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-08-23 17:13 UTC

No, that's impossible.  The PR in question was just opened -- no time for a merge conflict to have happened.

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-08-23 17:14 UTC

(Since literally nothing has been merged since it was opened)

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-08-23 17:15 UTC

Is the right thing to run a version that just discards any historical events first to clear the backlog?

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-08-23 17:16 UTC

I guess I don't totally trust GitHub to not send bogus merge-conflict notifications, which maybe could be what #6238 was?

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-08-23 17:20 UTC

So I think there's a few things we need to do:
* Add a bit of console logging when we're doing some action to the GitHub services on what the change is, including details like the timestamp of the notification from GitHub, etc., so that we can debug things like its response to #6238 after the fact and know that e.g. it was GitHub's fault.
* Add some sort of rate-limiting delay on our bot so we don't get stuck rate-limited all the time.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-08-23 17:52 UTC

Yeah, I'm really concerned about the random merge conflicts notifications we're getting. I'll check the payloads sent as this will be a problem with the API, not us.

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-08-23 17:52 UTC

Yeah, adding logging will help with a lot of this sort of debugging, since then we can go look at the logs to see what the cause was.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-08-23 17:53 UTC

Checked the API page again and noticed that not all merge conflict statuses are calculated for every PR so sometimes it returns null, which is why sometimes random pull requests get marked as conflicted. Fixing now

Interesting; is there a good way to add a recheck step?  Or do we just need to make sure it's never offline? :)

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-08-23 18:06 UTC

Payloads are meant to be designed to be sent immediately to reflect any real-time changes, so we can either just choose not to resend the payloads next time zulipbot goes down. But yes it's best to keep the server up 24/7

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-08-24 03:32 UTC

#117

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-08-24 03:41 UTC

Quoting my comment on that issue:
> There isn't an issue with duplicate payloads. The issue lies with the fact that payloads were delayed and once they were sent they no longer reflected the real-time data.

> Additionally, considering zulipbot operates on one payload at a time doesn't keep track of what payloads it has received, it's not very much in the interest of the project as we'd have to implement some form of persistent data storage. Looking to close this.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-09-07 19:38 UTC

the way we check for duplicates is we check for the content of the last comment on the PR; no comment is created if they're the same

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-09-10 04:56 UTC

@Yaw Anokwa Hello and welcome! Glad you decided to join us here :)

1. People will get assigned automatically when they claim an issue because zulipbot adds them as outside collaborators to your repository (the default permission is specified in `addCollaboratorPermission` in `src/config.js`; we use "read" permissions by default), so no need to worry about that :)

2. Currently we don't support environment variables yet, but I'd be happy to push a quick commit fixing that too

Repository Invitations API is now official
 August 23, 2017
The Repository Invitations API is now part of the official GitHub API. You will no longer be able to directly add a user to a repository. Instead, the user will receive an invitation, which they can accept or decline via email, notification, or API endpoint.
```

## Suppressing "needs review"

*2017-10-02 to 2017-10-02 · 5 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/Suppressing.20.22needs.20review.22/with/291016)*

**Craig Rodrigues** · 2017-10-02 16:12 UTC

I expressed interest in using zulipbot for the Twisted project.  One question I have is:

Is it possible to do a pull request, but in certain cases somehow suppress the application of the *needs review* tag?   For Twisted, sometimes people have a "Work In Progress" where they want to do a pull request in order to trigger some of the builders, just to see how they are doing, but they are not "done" yet, so don't want to submit for review just yet.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-10-02 16:32 UTC

Yep! Just specify the `reviewedLabel` and `needsReviewLabel` options as false and they won't be added to PRs :)

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-10-02 16:34 UTC

Alternatively, we have an option called `escapeWIPString` that won't label any PRs opened with the specified string in the PR title with the "needs review" label

**Craig Rodrigues** · 2017-10-02 18:44 UTC

Amazing!!

**Joshua Pan** · 2017-10-02 19:47 UTC

@Craig Rodrigues the best part about zulipbot is its ability to be configurable. Almost every single feature can be changed to suit the needs of the organization.

## graphql

*2017-10-12 to 2017-10-14 · 13 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/graphql/with/297317)*

**Greg Price** · 2017-10-12 17:30 UTC

GitHub has a GraphQL API  now (since barely a year ago, if that), which they've been building out quickly and are quite keen on being the future.

**Greg Price** · 2017-10-12 17:32 UTC

The big promise of GraphQL is that whatever data your app needs, you can query for *that*, in a SQL-like format  where you JOIN together the data you want -- so you can make 1 query rather than N+1 queries to get some information about some list of N different objects, like N pull requests or issues.

**Greg Price** · 2017-10-12 17:33 UTC

Which means your bot/app/whatever can run much faster, and can do much more before it hits API ratelimits.

**Greg Price** · 2017-10-12 17:35 UTC

Later today, there'll be a talk at GitHub Universe about the GraphQL API:
https://githubuniverse.com/program/#using-github-s-graphql-api-to-manage-open-source-projects
which looks pretty on point. Based on last year, the video should be posted online in a week or so.

**Greg Price** · 2017-10-12 17:37 UTC

Meanwhile, the API has [docs](https://developer.github.com/v4/), and there's a [blog post](https://githubengineering.com/the-github-graphql-api/) from last September (ok, so just over a year ago) announcing it and explaining the motivation.

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-10-12 17:41 UTC

I'd be curious for an analysis on whether it actually helps with the problems we have.

**Tim Abbott** · 2017-10-12 17:41 UTC

I imagine most of our API load is just doing a quick response to a webhook, but I don't know enough details to be sure.

**Rishi Gupta** · 2017-10-12 17:46 UTC

you can also watch it live at 4:50pm Pacific Time, at https://githubuniverse.com/program/

**Greg Price** · 2017-10-12 17:55 UTC

Yeah! @Cynthia Lin @Joshua Pan ^

**Greg Price** · 2017-10-12 17:57 UTC

At 4pm PT, there's another talk related to the GitHub API:
https://githubuniverse.com/program/#quickly-build-a-collaborative-cms-powered-by-the-github-api
This one isn't obviously directly relevant to our needs, but it sounds like they've put GitHub through some pretty impressive contortions by building on it with the API.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-10-12 18:22 UTC

I have a tennis match afterschool so I probably can't watch it then, but I'd imagine that we'd have to refactor immensely and altogether abandon the library we use for handling requests

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-10-12 18:25 UTC

Not so sure about what issues or advantages it has to our current REST API system though. GraphQL is a relatively new technology and there isn't that much documentation available for it

**Rein Zustand** · 2017-10-14 07:46 UTC

@Cynthia Lin  GraphQL is based on logic programming (i.e. GOFAI (which I am a huge fan of), query knowledge base, semantic web). I think it is totally worth the investment to try replacing some of the REST queries with a fewer, but more apt GraphQL statements.

## zulipbot review

*2017-12-07 to 2017-12-07 · 9 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/zulipbot.20review/with/369596)*

**Rishi Gupta** · 2017-12-07 08:22 UTC

Currently PRs get automatically marked as "needs review" if you push anything at all. Could we instead have things stay "reviewed" until the contributor does `@zulipbot review` or similar?

Likewise, I think the automatic tagging from "needs review" to "reviewed" is not currently helpful, since there are a lot of false positives (i.e. leaving a single comment flips the state). Maybe we could try manually tagging for a while, and see how that feels?

**Rohitt Vashishtha** · 2017-12-07 08:51 UTC

About the latter, we have three states for https://developer.github.com/v3/activity/events/types/#pullrequestreviewevent

`state: "approved", "open" and (not in the samples so not sure) "changes requested"`. We could trigger the `needs review -> reviewed` switch if the state is approved or changes requested.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-12-07 15:34 UTC

What Rohitt said is what we currently do, but I can easily add a command to manually change from needs review -> reviewed in a normal comment

**Rohitt Vashishtha** · 2017-12-07 15:48 UTC

Ah I'm sorry for the repetition then. I haven't taken a deep look at the source but just generally interested in the project.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-12-07 15:56 UTC

Thanks for your interest :)

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-12-07 15:56 UTC

So here's what we currently do:
* `needs review` gets added when the PR is first created
* `needs review` switches to `reviewed` when someone submits a review — whether it be approved, request changes, or a general review; doesn't trigger on *normal* PR comments
* `reviewed` switches back to `needs review` when someone pushes new changes to their branch

@Rishi Gupta , are you asking you add a command to mark a PR as reviewed through a command in a comment, or something else?

**Rohitt Vashishtha** · 2017-12-07 16:14 UTC

> or a general review; 

@Cynthia Lin I was suggesting removing this from the flipping logic. That is,  change the review state only if someone *decidedly* rejects or approves a PR.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-12-07 16:47 UTC

We rarely give out approve/reject reviews though; most of our reviews are the normal ones, with a bunch of comments on how to improve code

**Rishi Gupta** · 2017-12-07 18:11 UTC

oh cool, I didn't realize the second bullet point. I almost always submit a review (rather than giving individual comments), but it makes sense that for non-real reviews I should use the other mechanism.

I think we should remove the third bullet point, and replace that with an explicit action by the contributor (via a command in a comment). A lot of people push things that aren't ready for re-review (e.g. because that's how they run their tests), and we currently encourage people to post a comment like "updated, ready for review" anyway when they are ready for re-review.

## feature request: size

*2017-12-08 to 2017-12-18 · 19 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/feature.20request.3A.20size/with/371494)*

**Rishi Gupta** · 2017-12-08 00:00 UTC

Would it be possible for zulipbot to automatically label PRs with how "big" they are, based on the number of lines of code that were changed? E.g. `size: S`, `size: M`, etc.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-12-08 00:52 UTC

Definitely; do you have certain thresholds in mind?

**Rishi Gupta** · 2017-12-08 07:08 UTC

Maybe: 
XS for <= 5
S for <= 25
M for <= 50
L for <= 100
XL for > 100
but .. not including tests? Is that possible/easy?

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-12-08 07:15 UTC

Why do you plan on excluding tests? I'm planning to calculate based on PRs additions + deletions, so it might be hard to filter out which lines aren't a part of tests

**Rishi Gupta** · 2017-12-08 07:16 UTC

does zulipbot have access to the whole commit, or the whole diff?

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-12-08 07:19 UTC

It has access to all PR files modified here: https://developer.github.com/v3/pulls/#list-pull-requests-files

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-12-08 07:20 UTC

So it'd be possible to have something like `excludeFiles: ['frontend_tests', 'zerver/tests']` in the config

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-12-08 07:21 UTC

What if a PR only modifies tests though?

**Rishi Gupta** · 2017-12-08 07:23 UTC

cool! If it only modifies tests, then `size: XS`. In general modifications to tests are relatively fast to merge (even if there are many lines of change), since they rarely need manual testing, design decisions, etc.

**Greg Price** · 2017-12-08 19:43 UTC

I think in my ideal version of this, test changes would be included but with a discount -- like they'd only count for 50%, or 20%.

**Greg Price** · 2017-12-08 19:45 UTC

Sometimes a change is e.g. writing a whole new body of tests, or adding/changing some test infrastructure and refactoring a lot of tests -- and then that definitely takes more work to review than XS.

**Rishi Gupta** · 2017-12-08 20:15 UTC

yeah, agreed. I was trying to think of what would not be too crazy for configuration. But maybe this is a necessary level of complexity.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-12-08 20:48 UTC

So I'm a bit hesitant on whether it'd be okay to use a set constant for counting the weight of the excluded file changes.

Would something like bumping the threshold to the next level for excluded files be better? Ex) XS = 25 instead of 5, S = 50 instead of 25, etc

**Rishi Gupta** · 2017-12-08 21:40 UTC

hm, I think that will be hard to wrap our heads around (i.e. the way I naturally think about things is in this discount-y way). But for a V1 I think we could also do this without a discount (or without excluded files at all), and see how it goes.

**Greg Price** · 2017-12-09 00:34 UTC

Yeah, ==Rishi -- 80% of the value here will be in a V1, with or without any features for nuances like discounting test-code changes.

**Neil Pilgrim (neiljp)** · 2017-12-17 20:42 UTC

Would this be more appropriate as 'impact [to merge]'? The intent appears to be more about 'difficulties of merging'? ('merge difficulty'?)

**Rishi Gupta** · 2017-12-17 21:55 UTC

I think impact is harder to do automatically. This is more just "how much code will the reviewer be reading".

**Neil Pilgrim (neiljp)** · 2017-12-18 09:50 UTC

I agree if simply LOC, just not if eg tests are adjusted.

**Greg Price** · 2017-12-18 20:49 UTC

The reason I'd like an adjustment for test code (though, again, a 1.0 without anything like that will have most of the value already) is that as a reviewer I don't need to scrutinize the test code as closely. (E.g. because the potential impact of something wrong there is lower because it doesn't run in production or with real user data.) So reading N lines of test code is like reading N/2 or N/5 or something lines of app code, in the reviewing effort it requires.

hm, how about #6534? The last commit at #6591 does seem to reference it?

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-12-12 02:07 UTC

**Rishi Gupta** · 2017-12-12 03:35 UTC

adding a screenshot, since I'm about to review it and flip the state :)

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-12-12 04:12 UTC

#6591 - no, it did not; "this is a fix for #6951" isn't a valid keyword

**Rishi Gupta** · 2017-12-12 04:37 UTC

* #7440 - okay, makes sense. Would it be easy to change this behavior? I think it doesn't make sense to bug the contributor, when it is the reviewers' fault it is inactive. 
* #6591 - we seem to be talking past each other for some reason, so if the following doesn't clarify things we can just wait till it happens again, if it does at all :). I see that the github comment on the PR has "this is a fix for .." which is not a valid keyword, and is correctly ignored by zulipbot. However, I believe the commit message in one of the commits on that PR does have "Fixes #6951".

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-12-12 05:15 UTC

#6591 - **Both** PR description and PR commits need to reference the issue :)

**Cynthia Lin** · 2017-12-12 05:16 UTC

**Rohitt Vashishtha** · 2018-04-14 21:04 UTC

Hey! I got an inactivity warning for #387. Do we track individual commits to a PR or is it not possible with the GitHub API?

**Cynthia Lin** · 2018-04-14 21:05 UTC

We only track them if you have a PR open for it, so it's best to open a WIP PR :)

**Rohitt Vashishtha** · 2018-04-14 21:09 UTC

It's open: #8953.

**Rohitt Vashishtha** · 2018-04-14 21:10 UTC

As in, technically not specifically for this issue, but a comment on that PR had mentioned this issue and then a commit was added mentioning this issue on the existing PR.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2018-04-14 21:18 UTC

Ah, so the current system assigns one PR to one issue, so if you reference multiple issues in one PR, the later ones won't get picked up on.

## help wanted bug

*2018-02-09 to 2018-03-19 · 21 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/help.20wanted.20bug/with/489755)*

**Rishi Gupta** · 2018-02-09 04:28 UTC

Contributor here was able to claim #7830, even though there isn't a help wanted label.

**Joshua Pan** · 2018-02-09 04:38 UTC

I'm pretty sure the contributor isn't a first-time contributor and had commits pushed before?

**Rishi Gupta** · 2018-02-09 05:06 UTC

ah. okay, let's just make the rule "no one can claim an issue without `help wanted`".

**Tim Abbott** · 2018-02-09 19:08 UTC

@Rishi Gupta I'm not sure that rule makes sense; we might want a threshhold that's more like 20 commits.

**Tim Abbott** · 2018-02-09 19:08 UTC

Otherwise, even our core contributors will need full write access to the repo to claim issues.

**Rishi Gupta** · 2018-02-09 19:19 UTC

just for issues not labeled `help wanted`. 
I think of `help wanted` as conveying something like: we have a design for it, there is consensus this a good idea, and that if you work on it and do a good job it will be merged.

If those are not met, I don't think it makes sense for core contributors to work on it either, without chatting about it on czo first.

**Tim Abbott** · 2018-02-09 19:27 UTC

I think in practice, a lot of issues that someone who's been involved with the project for a while don't have the "help wanted" label, so I'm worried this could end up being annoying.

**Tim Abbott** · 2018-02-09 19:27 UTC

(I don't think we've been super consistent about adding that label to everything that would need it given that model)

**Rishi Gupta** · 2018-02-09 20:08 UTC

okay. A less extreme thing then might be:
* less than 20 commits you can't
* more than 20 commits zulipbot posts a warning, like "double check that if you work on this it will get merged"

**Tim Abbott** · 2018-02-09 20:34 UTC

I think we at most need the first (and I suspect that it just won't happen).  There aren't that many people who have >20 commits, and they mostly know to discuss before working on something complicated.  Can you open an issue in the zulipbot repo?

**Joshua Pan** · 2018-02-10 06:32 UTC

[#148](https://github.com/zulip/zulipbot/issues/148)

**VibhorGupta** · 2018-02-20 11:34 UTC

Does 20 commits here mean 20 commits in Zulip repos?

**Tim Abbott** · 2018-02-20 17:29 UTC

Yeah, something like that.  It's a rough concept.

**VibhorGupta** · 2018-02-22 18:14 UTC

But the 20 commit limit will be a good idea if the `help wanted` label is actively issued

**VibhorGupta** · 2018-03-12 05:54 UTC

Sir, as I see it, the 20 commit limit is going to restrict contributors from claiming even the most basic issues (in case help wanted label is missing on those issues), which deserve little tweaks in the code.
Right now, claiming issues without `help wanted` simply posts a warning. A way to solve this problem might be an introduction of a new label, called `claimable` which would be added only when the discussion on the respective issue has been resolved and a consensus is reached.

So, a new issue is opened, without the label. It is discussed, a consensus is reached, and a restricted set of people (probably the core contributors) type in a command like `@zulipbot mark claimable` after which the label is added. Zulipbot checks for this label everytime someone tries to claim an issue, and accordingly assigns or rejects the request depending on its presence

@Cynthia Lin @Tim Abbott

**VibhorGupta** · 2018-03-12 06:01 UTC

In fact, the 20 commit limit can be set for issuing the command `@zulipbot mark claimable`

**Tim Abbott** · 2018-03-12 15:26 UTC

We haven't been super consistent about using "help wanted" on new issues recently, so there is a problem we need to work on here.

**VibhorGupta** · 2018-03-12 16:50 UTC

Probably then we could take `help wanted` out of the equation? Just use the `claimable` label,  that too regulated by zulipbot itself. One can claim an issue only if it is marked `claimable`. I feel that claimable label can replace the help wanted label more or less. We could implement this for issues henceforth

That's what I proposed too, to use that label after a consensus is reached and then make an issue claimable

**Rohitt Vashishtha** · 2018-03-19 11:02 UTC

On #8149 zulipbot tries to avoid new contributors claiming the issue because 'help wanted' label is missing. But the reply implies that either of 'help wanted' or 'good first issue' should be there.

Inconsistent behavior.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2018-03-19 15:47 UTC

We just updated zulipbot to not allow force claiming at all, so a new reply should come up next time.

**Tim Abbott** · 2018-03-19 15:53 UTC

Yeah, I think if it has "good first issue", we should treat it as though it also has "help wanted"

## #Z145

*2018-02-18 to 2018-02-20 · 7 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/.23Z145/with/505890)*

**Cynthia Lin** · 2018-02-18 18:01 UTC

@Joshua Pan Do you have the source of the request for #Z145?

**Cynthia Lin** · 2018-02-20 18:05 UTC

Pinging @Rishi Gupta as well, since I assume you requested the feature in the first place

**Rishi Gupta** · 2018-02-20 18:14 UTC

yeah, it was me

**Cynthia Lin** · 2018-02-20 18:47 UTC

Do you have a link to the discussion where you originally requested it? Would appreciate it for issue tracking purposes

**Rishi Gupta** · 2018-02-20 18:56 UTC

hm, I don't see it in the obvious place (this stream). Are you not sure it's the right behavior?

**Cynthia Lin** · 2018-02-20 19:49 UTC

No, it's a new feature that you're requesting, so I'd like some background info for the issue context

**Rishi Gupta** · 2018-02-20 20:25 UTC

Sure. The main issue is that new users will zulipbot claim an issue that has already been claimed, and then the person who is working on it has to go and say "hey, new-user, I'm working on this", which is sort of an awkward interaction. It's especially awkward I think when the old user has a completed PR, that's just waiting for review. E.g. sometimes they'll submit a PR, wait a week, and the first notification they get about it is someone else claiming their issue.

I also don't know of any cases in the last few months where the correct thing was for multiple people to be able to claim an issue. 

It's possible this is a problem that was more pronounced during GCI. So if there is a limited time budget to implement features, I would go with any features that Tim asks for over this.

## msg to close issue in commit msg

*2018-04-07 to 2018-04-07 · 26 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/msg.20to.20close.20issue.20in.20commit.20msg/with/556286)*

**Rein Zustand** · 2018-04-07 08:00 UTC

https://github.com/zulip/zulip/pull/9011#issuecomment-379401468 has the caveat that if a commit is amended and force-pushed, it will create a new notification line on the issue page. Over time this will accumulate a mountain. See e.g. https://github.com/zulip/zulip/pull/450. I think it is better to put the command to close an issue in the PR message instead.

@Rein Zustand What do you mean to put the command to close an issue in the PR message?

**Joshua Pan** · 2018-04-07 17:56 UTC

> @Rein Zustand What do you mean to put the command to close an issue in the PR message?
By 'PR message', I meant, the comment thread on the PR that lives on gh only.

**Joshua Pan** · 2018-04-07 17:59 UTC

**Rein Zustand** · 2018-04-07 17:59 UTC

So I see it as a trade-off between being able to auto-close but with cluttered notification vs the other way around.

**Rein Zustand** · 2018-04-07 18:00 UTC

If I put the 'Fixes' in the commit message, then when I amended the commit to a new hash, then force push the commit, this adds a new notification line to the issue every time.

**Rein Zustand** · 2018-04-07 18:02 UTC

*2019-01-21 to 2019-02-09 · 21 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/New.20contributor.20definition/with/684572)*

**Neil Pilgrim (neiljp)** · 2019-01-21 06:48 UTC

I've not used zulipbot too much, but what is the threshold for contribution? I merged a PR from one contributor today, but they just tried to claim another PR and zulipbit is not happy since it doesn't have help-wanted or good-first-issue. Does zulipbot update a database over some time-frame, or is there a certain threshold?

**Joshua Pan** · 2019-01-21 08:09 UTC

**Neil Pilgrim (neiljp)** · 2019-01-21 16:38 UTC

@Sumanth Rao Does this explain your situation?

**Sumanth Rao** · 2019-01-22 00:27 UTC

I have accepted the invite and signed up to contribute to zulip repo around End last year.  But i had never accepted an invite from `zulip-terminal ` repository and it was my first contribution to it. So i guess that is the reason `@zulipbot` did not allow me to claim this. Does this make sense?

**Joshua Pan** · 2019-01-22 22:36 UTC

No, that invite was to the whole organization (not repo-based). pinging @Cynthia Lin for more info.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2019-01-23 00:36 UTC

The invite to collaborate is tailored for each repository. Sumanth is correct.

**Neil Pilgrim (neiljp)** · 2019-01-23 00:46 UTC

@Cynthia Lin What are the rules for that? People can claim help-wanted tasks one at a time until accepting the invite?

**Cynthia Lin** · 2019-01-23 00:50 UTC

So for someone who's contributing first time:
1. They need to comment `@zulipbot claim` on an issue with the help wanted/good first issue label
2. zulipbot posts a comment with an invite to the repository of the issue they tried to claim for the user
3. User is supposed to accept the invite, then zulipbot adds them as an assignee to the issue. They can't claim any other issues at this time.
4. User makes a PR, commit gets merged into the repository.
5. The user can claim an unlimited amount of issues without help wanted/good first issue labels at this point.

**Neil Pilgrim (neiljp)** · 2019-01-23 01:39 UTC

I think it is 5 that didn't work for @Sumanth Rao, ie on a second issue.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2019-01-23 01:50 UTC

I guess the reason that happened was he didn't previously accept the invite before tackling his next issue (step 3)

**Neil Pilgrim (neiljp)** · 2019-01-23 01:55 UTC

Aha!

**Sumanth Rao** · 2019-01-23 02:12 UTC

@Cynthia Lin Yes i didn't happen to accept the invite before as i did not claim it.  However @Neil Pilgrim  did assign me to the issue ( Issue being #247) and this did not result in the first-time contribution  invite by zulipbot. Does zulipbot give the invite if you claim it yourself?

**Joshua Pan** · 2019-01-23 02:13 UTC

It should.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2019-01-23 02:14 UTC

Yes, you would've only been able to receive and accept the invite whenever you used the `@zulipbot claim` for the firs ttime

**Sumanth Rao** · 2019-01-23 02:15 UTC

Ah okay. Thanks for the clarification. :+1:

## Locking issues

*2020-03-29 to 2020-03-31 · 9 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/Locking.20issues/with/842968)*

**Neil Pilgrim (neiljp)** · 2020-03-29 00:45 UTC

I've not considered a need to lock topics before, but recently wondered whether if an issue (or PR?) has further-discussion-required (or some other specified label), that it could be set to auto-lock and redirect the user to chat about it on czo or other location.

Does that seem reasonable, or something which would limit feedback too much from those who prefer a github interface?

**Tim Abbott** · 2020-03-29 18:32 UTC

I think it depends on the situation.  What problem are you trying to solve here?

**Neil Pilgrim (neiljp)** · 2020-03-29 20:15 UTC

I'm treating that label as meaning "don't just start work on this, but discuss it first". That doesn't mean that with just a GH workflow (little/no other chat platform/email/etc) the discussion couldn't happen on the issue itself, but rather it's a way of aiding the redirection of discussion elsewhere. Perhaps it's also separating issues into 'this is a thing to solve, we should do it, and we know how' vs cases where it's less clear, and people dive in.

**Neil Pilgrim (neiljp)** · 2020-03-29 20:16 UTC

That's probably more important on the bigger repos, but sometimes I want to file something to avoid forgetting it, but it's not something for people to just work on.

**Greg Price** · 2020-03-30 18:45 UTC

I sometimes file issues like that too.

I think just saying that in text in the issue can communicate the status fairly well -- people don't always read and understand that, but I think the people who don't see the text are likely not to understand from a label + auto-lock either.

**Neil Pilgrim (neiljp)** · 2020-03-31 00:43 UTC

It would be more personal, but the bot could post a standard message associated with the label, rather than relying on extra author text each time or users understanding labels.

**Greg Price** · 2020-03-31 19:25 UTC

If you want to post a standard message, GitHub has a direct solution for that:
https://help.github.com/en/github/writing-on-github/about-saved-replies

One useful aspect of that solution is that it keeps it super low-friction to edit the message slightly before sending, or add some other information to it. It's also lower friction to go edit the standard message than it would be if you needed to make a change to zulipbot to do it.

**Neil Pilgrim (neiljp)** · 2020-03-31 19:30 UTC

Yeah, I'm aware of that and thought of it as an alternative solution later after writing the message. Does that work across authors? (ie. multiple contributors can have the same messages available). I've no idea how zulipbot runs or is applied to repos right now, I just wondered about the feature.

**Greg Price** · 2020-03-31 20:10 UTC

My read of the docs is that the set of saved replies is all per-user.

There are definitely situations where I'd miss the ability to have a shared repertoire of them for a whole team, but actually in a Zulip context I think I'd tend to want my own version anyway :wink: And it's pretty easy for the next person to copy-paste a message into their own repertoire of saved replies.

*2020-04-20 to 2025-04-12 · 18 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/.E2.9C.94.20claiming.20issue/with/858352)*

**Sahil Batra** · 2020-04-20 17:12 UTC

@Cynthia Lin FYI.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2020-04-20 17:27 UTC

Can you give a link to the issues you were trying to claim?

**Sahil Batra** · 2020-04-20 17:28 UTC

[#14310](https://github.com/zulip/zulip/issues/14310) and [#14409](https://github.com/zulip/zulip/issues/14409)

**Julia Bichler** · 2021-12-10 20:28 UTC

I tried claiming the issue https://github.com/zulip/zulip/issues/19519 today, but i couldn't because the zulipbot said that i am a new contributer.
I already got two PRs merged though, and @Alya Abbott told me that i should now be able to claim multiple issues, so could you maybe help me with that?
Thanks in advance!

**Tim Abbott** · 2021-12-10 20:39 UTC

This is probably a bug in zulipbot.  Ultimately our plans involve deleting the existing implementation and replacing it with @Priyansh Garg (garg3133)'s rewrite.  Perhaps we should check whether the new implementation handles this correctly?

**Tim Abbott** · 2021-12-10 20:40 UTC

I believe what it tries to do is check whether you have past contributions according to GitHub's APIs; I wonder if there's some pagination issue preventing that.

**Julia Bichler** · 2021-12-10 20:53 UTC

oh okay, good to know!
Is there anything i can do to help find the issue with this?

**Julia Bichler** · 2022-07-01 08:01 UTC

hey everyone, i just wanted to check in again, since i still have the issue that i can't claim any issues without the 'help wanted' label (even though i already have some PRs merged).
Is there any progress about this?

**Alya Abbott** · 2022-07-05 19:24 UTC

We have actually changed the zulipbot rule to disallow claiming issues without "help wanted" for everyone. If you know there's an issue without that label that it makes sense for you to work on, you can just comment on it, and I'll assign it to you when I get a chance.

**Alya Abbott** · 2022-07-05 19:25 UTC

Issues without "help wanted" are often not ready to be worked on for one reason or another, so it's just easier to manage things that way.

**Julia Bichler** · 2022-07-07 12:30 UTC

@Alya Abbott oh okay, that's good to know :)

**Aditya Kasaudhan** · 2024-09-01 19:47 UTC

From my understanding of the issue, we can check the number of commits made by the user trying to claim the issue. If the user has more than one commit, we can configure Zulipbot to lift the restriction accordingly.

**Varun Kolanu** · 2025-04-10 18:44 UTC

Hey! I've opened a PR #Z242 which fixes this issue. Thanks Aditya for giving a perspective!

**Tim Abbott** · 2025-04-10 22:00 UTC

That does look like a nice fix, I'm happy to merge once it's confirmed it's been tested.

**Varun Kolanu** · 2025-04-11 03:21 UTC

Yeah I have tested it by locally setting up the bot and claiming issues on a test repo.

**Tim Abbott** · 2025-04-12 00:52 UTC

OK, merged.

**Notification Bot** · 2025-04-12 04:02 UTC

## Mentioning issue in commit description.

*2020-09-11 to 2020-09-11 · 8 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/Mentioning.20issue.20in.20commit.20description.2E/with/1013623)*

**Abhijeet Bodas** · 2020-09-11 05:17 UTC

I opened this pull request today, with just one commit. I have mentioned the relevant issue id in the commit description like so-
[image.png](https://chat.zulip.org/user_uploads/2/e9/FrdTsEfYyaf9EUg3a0YJNcWp/image.png) 

However, zulipbot posted this on opening the PR:
[image.png](https://chat.zulip.org/user_uploads/2/9a/vxwDFCquJjyandedZeU_7x6w/image.png) 

Am I missing something here or is this a bug?

**Anders Kaseorg** · 2020-09-11 05:37 UTC

zulipbot is correct. Your PR description says “Fixes #15963”, but your commit message only says “Part of #15963”.

**Abhijeet Bodas** · 2020-09-11 05:42 UTC

So must one specifically use the word "Fixes" so that the PR will get automatically closed?

**Cyphase** · 2020-09-11 05:44 UTC

Yes (along with some other variations). Otherwise you might accidentally close something when you only meant to reference it.

**Abhijeet Bodas** · 2020-09-11 05:49 UTC

Ok, thanks!

**Anders Kaseorg** · 2020-09-11 05:52 UTC

Or if it really only addresses part of the problem, “part of” is more accurate, but then the PR should also say “part of”.

**Abhijeet Bodas** · 2020-09-11 06:01 UTC

I intend the PR to fix the issue completely, but this particular commit works on only one task of the issue (out of 4 independent tasks).

**Greg Price** · 2020-09-11 08:02 UTC

In that case a good style is to have the last commit, the one that finishes the issue off, say "Fixes".

## zulipbot setup

*2021-01-24 to 2021-08-12 · 152 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/zulipbot.20setup/with/1106011)*

**Priyansh Garg (garg3133)** · 2021-01-24 20:42 UTC

I was trying to set up zulipbot locally  by following https://github.com/zulip/zulipbot/wiki/Installation, but the documentation is just too much confusing and I'm unable to set it up.
 
* For one thing, I'm having a very hard time in understanding which thing is to be done on which GitHub accounts/repositories. I currently have three places
1. The new GitHub account which will be used by zulipbot.
1.  The repository where the code for zulipbot resides.
1.  The repository where the bot is to be used.
And as a person who is not much experienced with bots, I found really difficult to figure out on which repository we need to add webhooks, which username and password to save in env variables of zulipbot's server.

* On the [Installation wiki page](https://github.com/zulip/zulipbot/wiki/Installation), it is mentioned that we should host the bot on Heroku/AWS and hosting it locally is discouraged but in the installation section of [Contributing.md](https://github.com/zulip/zulipbot/blob/master/.github/CONTRIBUTING.md), the only method mentioned is of hosting the bot locally.

* The installation guide on Installation wiki page and Contributing.md don't match. There are some extra things in Contributing.md, like **Create a GitHub App** (but not actually specifying how to set up one, like what settings to use).

* The following point on Installation wiki page doesn't specify that we also need to append `/github` in the URL.
> Enter your server's URL in the Payload URL field.

* I couldn't find any guide on how to actually deploy the bot on Heroku/AWS.

* It is nowhere mentioned that we actually need to create a `config.js` file before deploying. The instructions mentioned at [Configuration wiki page](https://github.com/zulip/zulipbot/wiki/Configuration) implies that we only need to create one if we don't want to use the default setting and thus, creating it is optional.
Also, `config.js` is added to `.gitignore` so how are we supposed to push it to the server so that the deployed application can use it? (I removed it from `.gitignore` but I'm not sure if this is the right way to do it.)

**Priyansh Garg (garg3133)** · 2021-01-24 20:44 UTC

Even after spending a lot of hours on this, I'm still unable to set it up correctly :/

**Tim Abbott** · 2021-01-25 22:31 UTC

@Cynthia Lin @Joshua Pan may be able to help.

**Cynthia Lin** · 2021-01-26 01:08 UTC

Hey @Priyansh Garg , thanks for your interest in zulipbot!
> it is mentioned that we should host the bot on Heroku/AWS and hosting it locally is discouraged but in the installation section of Contributing.md, the only method mentioned is of hosting the bot locally.
> The installation guide on Installation wiki page and Contributing.md don't match.

Contributing.md is detailing the **development** process for contributing code to the project (new features, bug fixes, etc.) so if you're trying to deploy your own instance into **production**, please feel free to ignore it!

> The following point on Installation wiki page doesn't specify that we also need to append /github in the URL.

Correct, that's an important step, thanks for catching the mistake! Updated.

> I couldn't find any guide on how to actually deploy the bot on Heroku/AWS.

It's been a while since I've deployed to Heroku since I operate off of VPSes now, but I believe a `git push heroku master` (or whatever your heroku remote is named) with the correct `config.js`/user account authentication set in the environment variables/`secrets.json` should automatically do the trick, since Heroku runs `npm start` by default which is indeed how you start the process. For AWS, the goal should be similar; to set up the necessary config/auth data on the server before running `npm start`.

> Configuration wiki page implies that we only need to create one if we don't want to use the default setting and thus, creating it is optional.

Might be a bug since intended behavior is to directly operate off of default.js, will investigate further

> how are we supposed to push it to the server so that the deployed application can use it?

If you're working off of services that require you to check in every file into Git to be deployed (sounds like Heroku) without giving you access to the server to write files directly to, it would be necessary to remove it from .gitignore, yes.

Hope this helps. Feel free to ask for clarification/more help if you wish!
