The code in `dist` directory is actually auto-generated using [vercel/ncc](https://github.com/vercel/ncc) as prescribed in [github-actions documentation](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/creating-actions/creating-a-javascript-action#commit-tag-and-push-your-action-to-github). As github-actions don't install any external dependencies while running on VM, we either need to push the `node_modules` as well with the code (which is generally not preferred and also takes up a lot of space) or compile all the dependencies into the code itself using tools like vercel/ncc (takes up much less space compared to `node_modules`).

All the code written by me resides in `src` directory.

**Anders Kaseorg** · 2021-02-22 19:11 UTC

`@vercel/ncc` should be added to `devDependencies` and used in a `build` script so that `dist` can be updated with `npm run build`.

**Greg Price** · 2021-03-04 23:22 UTC

That looks like a pretty reasonable structure!

I like the ability to customize templates using the repo's local config.

A few other thoughts looking at the interface (as seen in those config files):
* Let's use YAML for the config, not JSON. JSON is great for transferring data, but it's a bad format for configuration files (or anything else that's going to be maintained over time by humans). The absolutely fatal problem is that it doesn't permit comments.
* Let's make sure that everything has a reasonable default. (Maybe this is already true, and you're just demonstrating what options are available.) In particular, I'm not sure it makes sense to even have `max_issue_assignees` be configurable, but certainly it should default to 1.
* The workflow file is written as if it's just about claiming issues. But I think the idea is that there'll be one workflow corresponding to the bot as a whole, and it will take care of all the various commands -- right? So a good improvement to the demo would be to change the various names to reflect that.

**Tim Abbott** · 2021-03-05 00:22 UTC

Thanks for taking a look, @Greg Price!  I don't think I have the capacity to do early reviews or beta testing on zulip/zulip, so we should plan to do so on mobile or zulip-terminal.

**Greg Price** · 2021-03-05 01:13 UTC

Here's a few thoughts on the implementation:
* There's a lot of string-manipulation in that `parse_comment` function. That seems natural and appropriate for the job it has to do. But let's definitely have some unit tests for it.
  * One thing that will help with that is to split up what the function does: currently it both parses the comment, and also goes ahead and executes the commands. Instead, let's have a function that just parses, and returns a data structure describing what commands to run. Then the caller can go through and run the commands.
* I'm kind of puzzled by what's going on with `this`. Particularly `this.commands.get(keyword)` -- it looks like that's supposed to return the module from one of the files like `commands/claim.js`? Where does that `this.commands` come from?
  * Ah, I see, the `this` that gets passed around is prepared by `getClient()`.
* Instead of dynamically importing files based on `fs.readdirSync` of a directory, I think it'd be much better to explicitly import specific files by name.
  * So instead of that loop with the readdir and the ``require(`./commands/${file}`);``, just have an object literal like `{ claim: require('./commands/claim'), abandon: require('./commands/abandon') }`. Or perhaps better yet, do all the imports with `import` statements at the top of the file.
  * This is good because it makes it a lot more legible for people to trace what's going on -- and also for tools to do so, like IDEs and linters and type-checkers.
* I think all the `.apply` calls are a sign that the way `this` is being used here doesn't really align with the way the code is organized.
  * It's possible that there's a good way to organize this code in the kind of object-oriented style that that use of `this` is suggesting.
  * But probably the best immediate way to fix this is to just drop the use of `this`, and instead pass the client object around as a normal parameter, with a name like `client`. Just giving it a meaningful name like that will help significantly in understanding what's going on with it.

**Greg Price** · 2021-03-05 01:23 UTC

Relatedly: let's get some types on this code. :smile: I think that'd help significantly in understanding where the various bits of data are coming from and what's in them, and it's also likely to help find and prevent some bugs.

It looks like there's already a build step here (note that the `actions.yml` points at `dist/index.js`, not at the source file `index.js`), so this should be pretty straightforward to do. Here's someone's writeup of how to use TypeScript for writing a GitHub Action:
https://nils-braun.github.io/github-action/
The key section in that post is the one headed "Initialize the Action".

**Greg Price** · 2021-03-05 01:24 UTC

@Anders Kaseorg has worked on using TypeScript in our frontend repo, and may have some other advice to add on using TypeScript here.

**Priyansh Garg (garg3133)** · 2021-03-07 17:08 UTC

Here's the default configuration file of `bot-commands` action listing all the features and options that the action will provide: https://github.com/garg3133/zulipbot-action/blob/yaml-config/bot-commands/config/default-config.yaml

All these features are already available with zulipbot right now, I've just changes the structure of config file to make it more readable and documented all the features and options using comments.

One thing to note about this design is that all the commands provided by the action will be disabled by default. To enable a command, user can list that command along with a list of keywords that should execute that command, under the `commands` key. Also, if the user lists the command under the `commands` key but the list corresponding to that command is empty, the command will not work as there are no keywords to execute that command. This will make it easy for users to skip the features they don't wish to use, by just skipping the mention of that feature under `commands`.

**Greg Price** · 2021-03-09 02:44 UTC

Cool, thanks.

Let's arrange the syntax of this so that it doesn't make it a central thing to customize the names of the commands. Possibly at some point it'll make sense to have those be customizable -- but I think it's best if generally a given command has the same name in every repo that's using Zulipbot. That way when people are using it in several different repos, like the different repos of the Zulip project, they don't have to try to have in their head several different variations on the syntax.

**Greg Price** · 2021-03-09 02:45 UTC

**Greg Price** · 2021-03-09 02:54 UTC

Then I think another helpful step in thinking about this can be: let's consider some drafts of what an actual *live* config file might be, i.e. one that might appear in a repo that's using Zulipbot.

Here's one version:
```yaml
features:

  # (Enables @add and @remove, with default configuration.)
  labels: true

  # (Enables @claim and @abandon, plus provides some configuration.)
  claim:
    require_labels: ["good first issue", "help wanted"]

```

**Greg Price** · 2021-03-09 03:01 UTC

Several thoughts embedded in that draft:
* If we're enabling `@claim`, we'll want to enable `@abandon` at the same time, and vice versa. So, best to group the enabling of them into one setting.
* If we enable some cluster of commands, and also have some config for it, it's cleanest (for people writing and maintaining config files) if those happen together.

  We can allow that reasonably cleanly by a bit of overloading: accepting a boolean `true` for "yes please, enable, and just use default config", and a more general map/object for "yes, enable, and also here's some config details". Oh and I guess boolean `false` for an explicit "no, disable".

**Greg Price** · 2021-03-09 03:06 UTC

Oh, and a tiny one:
* Instead of `kebab-case`, it's a bit more convenient to name the keys with `snake_case` or `camelCase`. That's because the latter can be used with property syntax in JS, so you can say `config.some_property` rather than `config['some-property']`.

**Priyansh Garg (garg3133)** · 2021-03-10 20:17 UTC

> s/unallow/disallow/g

Done

> What's a "new contributor" exactly? This isn't clear in the existing Zulipbot's config either.

As per the implementation in the current version of Zulipbot, there are two versions of "new contributors":
* The `add_as_collaborator` and label-based warnings applies to only for those contributors who are not yet added as a collaborator on the repository (which is currently mandatory if using the claim feature). So, if a contributor is already a collaborator on the repository (no matter if he/she has actually contributed anything to the repository), he/she can claim any issue, irrespective of the label. 
* The `max_issue_claims` restriction applies to the contributors who are already a collaborator (which is obvious as a user has to become a collaborator first before getting the first issue assigned) but haven't yet committed anything on the repository (not present in GitHub's contributors list for the repository).

**Priyansh Garg (garg3133)** · 2021-03-10 20:45 UTC

> The for_labels which can be either positive or negative is confusing. Probably better to have two separate options, and just say they can't both be present. (Or if needed in the future we can extend the options to allow both kinds, with a way to specify precedence between them -- if so it should probably just allow any sequence of "allow if this label; else forbid if this label; else allow if this label; …".)

How about changing the language a bit so that it does not feel that confusing?
Instead of using:
```
      # Warn new contributors and disallow them from claiming
      # the issues which match the below mentioned conditions.
      # Don't mention to use default config.
      warn_and_disallow_claim:

        # Labels for which new contributors should be warned
        # and disallowed from claiming the issue.
        # @default: [] (empty list)
        for_labels: ["good first issue", "help wanted"]

        # Warn and disallow to claim on presence or on absence
        # of the above labels?
        # @default: true (disallow on presence of above labels)
        on_presence: false
```
we can use:
```
      # Assign the issue to the new contributor only if it
      # contains:
      # - One or more of the below mentioned labels, if
      #   `are_present` is set to true;
      # - None of the below mentioned labels, if
      #   `are_present` is set to false;
      # Otherwise, generate a warning.
      assign_only_if:
        labels: ["good first issue", "help wanted"]
        are_present: true
```

With counter example:
```
      assign_only_if:
        labels: ["hard", "difficult"]
        are_present: false
```

> ``` # Assign the issue to the new contributor only if it # contains: # - One or more of the below mentioned labels, if # `are_present` is set to true; # - None of the below mentioned labels, if # `are_present` is set to false; # Otherwise, generate a warning. assign_only_if: labels: ["good first iss …

I think it's still kind of confusing. Also, it's quite ambiguous whether the requirement is that there's *any* of those labels, or *all* of them. (The doc comment explains, but in an actual config file with just the config itself it'd be ambiguous.)

So another version would be:
```yaml
# A list of rules to decide whether to allow the new contributor to claim the issue.
# The first matching rule controls the outcome.
assign_only_if:
  - label: "good first issue"
  - label: "help wanted"
  - false
```

Where in the other direction it'd be:
```yaml
assign_only_if:
  - not_label: hard
  - not_label: difficult
  - true
```

**Anders Kaseorg** · 2021-03-12 18:18 UTC

I assume the idea is that these translate directly to stepwise algorithms:

```python
if "good first issue" in labels: return True
if "help wanted" in labels: return True
return False
```

```python
if "hard" in labels: return False
if "difficult" in labels: return False
return True
```

How this works is whenever some area labels are added on an issue, it will edit the issue itself appending a message `CC by @zulipbot: area-teams...`at the end of the issue description. **Priyansh Garg (garg3133)** · 2021-03-23 20:27 UTC

For the Activity action, I don't feel the need of creating a separate configuration file, as all its configurations can be done in the workflow file itself.

Sample workflow file: https://github.com/garg3133/git-github-demo/blob/main/.github/workflows/activity-action.yml

In the above workflow file, I've included all the configurations related to issue activity, which are currently being used by Zulipbot. As we are currently not using activity feature in pull requests, I've only included the configuration which might be good to have in the action and left the rest which don't seems to be of much use. For ex., 

* Adding a `reviewed` label on a PR when someone reviews that PR doesn't make much sense (in Zulip atleast) as not everyone reviews the PR completely and it may be good to have multiple people review a PR.
* Automatically adding a `needs review` label on a PR whenever it gets updated also doesn't make sense as the PR might not be ready for review at that time. Instead, we can put it in our guidelines to add `needs review` label on the PR whenever it is ready for review.

But the following activity features for pull requests might be good to have:

* Don't post warnings on the issue related to inactivity if the linked PR still needs review (sometimes it might take a lot of time before a PR is reviewed but the bot starts posting warning on the issue and unassigns the issue even if the inactivity is not on the contributor's side). But for this to work as expected, reviewer who makes the final merge in the repository (and hence reviews the pull request completely) should remove the `needs review` label from that PR, otherwise if the contributor becomes inactive after the review and don't update the PR further, the issue will still remain assigned to him.
* Add the reviewers of a pull request as assignees on that PR -- Might be useful to some but not very sure about it.

Reference for all the PR related configurations mentioned above: https://github.com/zulip/zulipbot/wiki/Configuration#pull-requests-1

I was going through the code of `activity` feature of Zulipbot, trying to understand the logic (which seems pretty complicated and buggy at some places) and found that if some contributor has claimed an issue and been inactive for 10 days, a warning is posted on the issue that the user will be unassigned after 4 more days of inactivity. But if the contributor becomes active again after the warning and works for some days, expectedly, the contributor should get a warning again after 10 days and unassigned after 14 days of latest inactivity but the Zulipbot allows only 4 days of inactivity after that. But if that contributor is unassigned from the issue (after 14 days of inactivity) and he/she claims it again, Zulipbot will now allow 14 days of inactivity to that user.

I suppose the former case is a bug.

**Priyansh Garg (garg3133)** · 2021-04-08 22:30 UTC

Developed a prototype for activity action too which posts a warning comment on an issue if it has been inactive for more than `days_until_warning` days. If the issue is still inactive after the warning, it will be unassigned after `days_until_abandon` days. But if the contributor starts working again on the issue, the allowed inactivity before unassignment will again be reset to `days_until_warning + days_until_abandon` days (`days_until_abandon` are counted after the warning), thus fixing the bug I reported earlier [here](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/stream/105-zulipbot/topic/zulipbot.20setup/near/1150130).

Along with this, I've also included the configuration for two labels, one for issue and one for a linked PR, whose presence on an issue or PR will prevent that issue/linked issue from being checked by activity action. This may have a use case like if we want to keep an issue assigned to someone (for which we can use some label like "keep assigned") or if an issue is stale because of a pending review (for which we can use label like "needs review" on the linked PR).

Action: https://github.com/garg3133/zulipbot-action/tree/activity-action/activity-action
(I've tried some new things in this action this time, like using import statements instead or require, putting each function in a separate file, etc.)
Workflow file used: https://github.com/garg3133/git-github-demo/blob/main/.github/workflows/activity-action.yml

Tested on: 
(I have kept both warning and abandon time to be 6 hours (1 day in workflow and then divided the time by 4 in code) for testing purpose)

(Just normal warning and abandon)

https://github.com/garg3133/git-github-demo/issues/16

https://github.com/garg3133/git-github-demo/issues/5
(Linked PR has a `skip_issue_with_pull_label` label, so this issue is skipped by activity action)

**Priyansh Garg (garg3133)** · 2021-05-12 18:38 UTC

Created a prototype for the pulls-action as well. I've implemented the feature of size-labels for now.

Action: https://github.com/garg3133/zulipbot-action/tree/pulls-action/pulls-action
Config file used: https://github.com/garg3133/git-github-demo/blob/main/.github/config/pulls-action-config.yaml
Workflow file 1: https://github.com/garg3133/git-github-demo/blob/main/.github/workflows/pulls-action.yml
Workflow file 2: https://github.com/garg3133/git-github-demo/blob/main/.github/workflows/post-pulls-action.yml
Tested on: https://github.com/garg3133/git-github-demo/pull/21

Note: Pulls action requires two workflow files as the workflows triggered on the `pull_request` event do not get write permissions on the repository, so we cannot add labels or add comments to a pull request during that workflow run. So, we analyze the pull request during the first workflow run, save the results in an artifact and then use those results during the second workflow run (which is automatically triggered when the first workflow ends and have the write permissions on the repository) to add labels and comments on the pull requests.
More details here: https://securitylab.github.com/research/github-actions-preventing-pwn-requests/

**Tim Abbott** · 2021-06-07 20:10 UTC

@Priyank Patel would you be up for helping review the TypeScript?

**Priyank Patel** · 2021-06-07 20:35 UTC

Yes, I can take a look at the migration.

**Priyank Patel** · 2021-06-08 17:29 UTC

Here are a couple of notes:
- You should probably borrow strict type-checking options from the [tsconfig.json](https://github.com/zulip/zulip/blob/master/tsconfig.json#L23-L32) we have from the server repo.
- I don't think you need the `types.ts` you have at the root level. I think you can replace`OctokitClient` type with the `GitHub` variable you import instead.
- You don't need to type annotate every single variable, TypeScript is pretty good at interpreting types. In server repo, we only type annotate function variable and return type and let TypeScript interpret the variables. (We use the `noImplictAny` to make sure we don't let anything be interpreted as `any` which could be a problem. I bring this up because I think this will help with the issue where you have a bunch of types declared in `types.ts` and `interfaces.ts` files that we can avoid.
- I would avoid having a bunch of types declared in `types.ts` and `interfaces.ts` etc. unless it's really needed. You should instead inline the types and if you need to reuse them export them from the specific modules and import them when needed.

Since I am not too familiar with the code I don't have much to add. I think you are strongly typing annotating your code which is good.

**Priyansh Garg (garg3133)** · 2021-06-09 11:45 UTC

**Priyansh Garg (garg3133)** · 2021-07-13 22:06 UTC

I've currently set the configuration of this action to match with the original Zulipbot configuration. The complete configuration for this action is available [here](https://github.com/garg3133/zulipbot-action/blob/main/commands-action/config/sample-config.yml).

While most of the configuration options are almost the same in this action as the original Zulipbot, the main difference is in the `label` configuration where we can now set whom to give full permission of adding/removing any labels (might be the org members) and whom to give restricted permissions.

**Priyansh Garg (garg3133)** · 2021-07-13 22:07 UTC

Sample label config:

```yaml
# Enable @add and @remove label commands.
#
# Skip this config if you don't wish to use this feature.
label:
  # @keywords:
  #   "all": All authenticated users on GitHub.
  #   "author": Author of issue or pull request.
  #   "member": Member of the organization containing the
  #            repository.
  #   "@username": Specific GitHub user.

  # Users given full_permission can add or remove any labels on
  # issues and pull requests.
  full_permission:
    to: ["author", "member", "@garg3133"]

  # Users given restricted_permission can add or remove only
  # a few labels according to the restrictions specified below.
  restricted_permission:
    to: ["all"]
    # Mention either `allowed_labels` or `restricted_labels`,
    # do not mention both at the same time.
    allowed_labels: ["bug", "feature"]
    # restricted_labels: ["good first issue"]
```

**Priyansh Garg (garg3133)** · 2021-07-13 22:14 UTC

Additionally, there are a few steps that need to be followed for this action to work:
* Get the Personal access token for Zulipbot's GitHub account, as documented [here](https://github.com/garg3133/zulipbot-action/tree/main/commands-action#create-a-github-account-for-the-bot).
* Add Zulipbot as a collaborator on the repository with admin access (if this isn't already the case).
* Save the Personal access token of Zulipbot as a secret on the repository, with the name `BOT_ACCESS_TOKEN`, as documented [here](Save the Personal access token of Zulipbot as a secret on this repository, with the name BOT_ACCESS_TOKEN).

**Priyansh Garg (garg3133)** · 2021-07-14 06:48 UTC

I was thinking of removing the add_as_collaborator thing as it was originally used due to the limitations of GitHub API at that time which didn't allow to assign issues to the users who were not a collaborator on the repository. But it also has another use case of giving some instructions to the contributors who try to claim an issue for the first time, which seems to be important for us. I tried searching for a replacement for this use case but I couldn't find a way to get all the comments posted by a contributor across the repository, hence there's no way of knowing if a comment is the first from the user.

**Priyansh Garg (garg3133)** · 2021-07-14 06:54 UTC

There's a way to get all the issue comments across the repository, but that'll be just too many comments to handle and look through.

As an alternative, maybe we can just look through the issue comments from the past one month and give instructions to the contributor if he/she is a new contributor (do not have any commit on the repository) and is commenting on an issue for the first time since one month?

## Restrict who can add labels

*2021-03-25 to 2021-06-24 · 13 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/Restrict.20who.20can.20add.20labels/with/1142753)*

**Aman (amanagr)** · 2021-03-25 05:17 UTC

Can we restrict that only `members` of `Zulip` org can run commands like `@zulipbot add "good first issue"`?

**Tim Abbott** · 2021-03-25 08:13 UTC

Yeah, I think that'd be a good idea.

**Aman (amanagr)** · 2021-03-25 09:04 UTC

Opened #193 to track it.

**Priyansh Garg (garg3133)** · 2021-03-25 17:00 UTC

Instead of restricting them from adding a few labels, how about only allowing them to add a few labels because I think they are not supposed to add most of the labels on issues.

Same can be done in PRs (allowing them to add and remove "needs review" label only).

**Aman (amanagr)** · 2021-03-25 17:09 UTC

I don't think we want new members to add any labels to issues or pull requests. Allowing just members for this should be good enough.

**Adam Birds** · 2021-03-26 01:35 UTC

But there are also a lot of issues that don't get any labels added as it is currently. Restricting to members only may slow down a lot of issues getting picked up. I usually search for issues in the area I like to work in, but just after scrolling through a few pages, we've got issues raised from 2020 for some integration requests that haven't received a single label, which means they don't appear in the area correctly but also means that new contributors may end up with a shortage of `good first issue`issues to pick up.

**Priyansh Garg (garg3133)** · 2021-03-26 18:57 UTC

(replying to Aman Agrawal (amanagr))
> I don't think we want new members to add any labels to issues or pull requests. Allowing just members for this should be good enough.
Zulipbot doesn't allow new members or anyone as such to add labels on issues. Only the person who opened an issue can add labels to that issue using the `@zulipbot add "label-name"` command. And I think we should allow the issue authors to add some labels, if not all. 

What we can do additionally is restrict the new contributors from adding the labels on issues which they opened themselves.

**Tim Abbott** · 2021-03-27 00:15 UTC

(replying to Adam Birds)
> But there are also a lot of issues that don't get any labels added as it is currently. Restricting to members only may slow down a lot of issues getting picked up. I usually search for issues in the area I like to work in, but just after scrolling through a few pages, we've got issues raised from 20 …

I'm actually 1/3 of the way through the process of adding labels to everything without a label (https://github.com/zulip/zulip/issues?q=is%3Aissue+is%3Aopen+no%3Alabel), which I started earlier this week.  I think we should aim to not have many of those.

**Tim Abbott** · 2021-03-27 00:15 UTC

> What we can do additionally is restrict the new contributors from adding the labels on issues which they opened themselves.

We can also consider this just a social problem -- it's not that common that someone wants to add "good first issue" so they can claim something, and we could just tell them not to do that in the docs.

**Priyansh Garg (garg3133)** · 2021-06-22 18:30 UTC

For managing the permissions of who can add/remove labels using zulipbot commands in a better way, I'm thinking of having the following configuration options in zulipbot commands-action:

```yaml
# This is a sample configuration

# We can use some keywords to assign permissions to a group of people.
# "all" -- give permission to everyone
# "member" -- give permission to org members
# "author" -- give permission to issue/PR author
# "@username" -- give permission to a specific user
labels:
  full_permissions:
    to: ["member", "author", "@garg3133"]
  restricted_permissions:
    to: ["all"]
    # Exactly one of the two fields below should be mentioned at a time
    allowed_labels: ["needs-review", ...]
    restricted_labels: ["good first issue", ...]
```

Thoughts?

**Priyansh Garg (garg3133)** · 2021-06-24 04:02 UTC

@Aman Agrawal (amanagr) @Tim Abbott ?

**Aman (amanagr)** · 2021-06-24 04:29 UTC

We don't want everyone to add `"good first issue"` labels. We could allow labels like "bug", "question", "feature request" etc.

**Tim Abbott** · 2021-06-24 17:14 UTC

Yeah, "good first issue" actually requires a lot of knowledge about how Zulip is engineered to effectively tag.

## zulipbot warns on lack of fixes #x

*2021-04-18 to 2021-04-19 · 5 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/zulipbot.20warns.20on.20lack.20of.20fixes.20.23x/with/1165691)*

**Rein Zustand** · 2021-04-18 10:02 UTC

Not sure if I have reported this before, but this warning https://github.com/zulip/zulip-terminal/pull/998#issuecomment-821966254 says that I should put "fixes #x" in my commit message. Can it just detect that in my PR message, that I already say "fixes #x" and not complain about it?

**Priyansh Garg (garg3133)** · 2021-04-18 14:24 UTC

@Rein Zustand I think this functionality is for the reason that sometimes if the commits in a PR need some small changes before the PR gets merged, then the maintainers may rebase those commits and makes the changes themselves, due to which the PR does not show as being merged and as a result, the corresponding issue do not close. See #16301 for one instance.

Also, it may happen that a PR resolves multiple issues but due to being stuck at one last issue, all the other issues, for which the fixes have already been merged, won't get closed as the PR is still open. 

So, adding a `Fixes: #x` line in the commit message of the final commit for an issue helps close the linked issue whenever that final commit for the issue is merged into master. It is not necessary to add this line in all the commits but only in the last commit of the PR.

**Rein Zustand** · 2021-04-18 23:51 UTC

In some cases, even if the maintainers make modification to the PR, it is still marked as merged, e.g. https://github.com/zulip/zulip-archive/pull/48 (the commit message). Not saying that the "fixes #x" should be exclusive in PR messages, but it should also be acceptable that "fixes #x" can live in PR message instead of commit message.

For PR that resolves multiple issues, as a separate case, the "fixes #x" can be added in the commit messages.

**Anders Kaseorg** · 2021-04-19 00:56 UTC

Inspecting the history of the Git repository with `git log` or `git blame` or `gitk` or any other local tool will not show you PR messages. Those live entirely in GitHub-land.

**Rein Zustand** · 2021-04-19 01:08 UTC

OK that's enough reason to not put it in PR messages then.

## adding labels

*2022-02-01 to 2022-02-03 · 3 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/adding.20labels/with/1320025)*

**Alya Abbott** · 2022-02-01 01:51 UTC

Hey! I was wondering -- are there any restrictions on the `@username add "label name"` command? In particular, I'm happy to have folks adding some types of labels (e.g. area labels), but others should only be added by users with administrative privileges (e.g. "priority: high", "help wanted").

**Tim Abbott** · 2022-02-01 22:30 UTC

I'm not sure.  Certainly it's something that could be added if we were actively making changes to zulipbot.

**Priyansh Garg (garg3133)** · 2022-02-03 22:32 UTC

IIRC, in the current version of Zulipbot, only the authors of issues/PRs can use that command. I think it was actually meant to be used by anyone but due to some logic mistake in the code, only authors can use that command.

In the new version, I have included such permissions, which are completely customizable. Here's a sample config file for the same: https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/stream/105-zulipbot/topic/zulipbot.20setup/near/1230570.

## can't take issue except help wanted

*2022-05-25 to 2022-05-27 · 3 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/can.27t.20take.20issue.20except.20help.20wanted/with/1384391)*

**Rafid Aslam** · 2022-05-25 03:18 UTC

I'm trying to take an issue (https://github.com/zulip/zulip/issues/21789) with `@zulipbot claim`, somehow it doesn't allow me to take the issue except the ones that have 'help wanted` label, although currently I'm not working on any other issue

**Alya Abbott** · 2022-05-25 08:32 UTC

@Rafid Aslam as [mentioned in the contributor guide](https://zulip.readthedocs.io/en/latest/overview/contributing.html#in-the-main-server-and-web-app-repository), you can only claim "help wanted" issues with Zulipbot. This is to make sure folks don't accidentally pick up issues that are not ready to be worked on, or require specialized expertise, etc.

In this particular case, I think the "help wanted" label is missing accidentally, or perhaps because I thought it would be tracked on the `zulip-archive` side. In any case, I've gone ahead and assigned the issue to you. Thanks for picking it up!

**Rafid Aslam** · 2022-05-27 03:48 UTC

Thank you very much!

*2023-01-24 to 2023-01-26 · 4 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/helping.20contributors.20not.20claim.20PRs/with/1495650)*

**Alya Abbott** · 2023-01-24 06:49 UTC

On #23957 (and occasionally elsewhere), we have several contributors attempting to claim a PR. A couple of ideas for Zulipbot improvement that @Lauryn Menard and I had when we were discussing this: 

1. Not automatically adding the "good first issue" to PRs, since that label is probably why so many people are finding it.
2. Customizing the response to claim requests to explain that this is a PR, and not an issue. Currently it says:

> Hello `@user`!
> 
> Thanks for your interest in Zulip! You have attempted to claim an issue without the label "help wanted". You can only claim and submit pull requests for issues with the [help wanted](https://github.com/zulip/zulip/issues?q=is%3Aopen+is%3Aissue+no%3Aassignee+label%3A%22help+wanted%22+sort%3Aupdated-desc) label.
> 
> If this is your first time here, we recommend reading our [guide for new contributors](https://zulip.readthedocs.io/en/latest/overview/contributing.html) before getting started.

Thoughts?

**Tim Abbott** · 2023-01-25 20:53 UTC

Yeah, customizing the text for PRs seems helpful.

**Alya Abbott** · 2023-01-26 22:25 UTC

OK, how about something like this?

> Hello `@user`!  You have attempted to claim a [pull request](https://docs.github.com/en/pull-requests/collaborating-with-pull-requests/proposing-changes-to-your-work-with-pull-requests/about-pull-requests), not an issue. To learn how to find an issue to work on,  check out our [guide for new contributors](https://zulip.readthedocs.io/en/latest/contributing/contributing.html#picking-an-issue-to-work-on).

**Alya Abbott** · 2023-01-26 22:26 UTC

@Anders Kaseorg would you be up for helping with this when you have time?

## 10 day automated message

*2023-03-09 to 2023-04-25 · 12 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/10.20day.20automated.20message/with/1521488)*

**Lauryn Menard** · 2023-03-09 11:02 UTC

zulipbot sends the below message when there haven't been recent updates on an assigned/claimed issue.

> We noticed that you have not made any updates to this issue or linked PRs for 10 days. Please comment here if you are still actively working on it. Otherwise, we'd appreciate a quick @zulipbot abandon comment so that someone else can claim this issue and continue from where you left off.
>
> If we don't hear back, you will be automatically unassigned in 4 days. Thanks!

**Lauryn Menard** · 2023-03-09 11:02 UTC

I feel like the "If we don't hear back" phrase at the end is a bit confusing because, unless I'm missing something, there is no comment a person can make on the issue that will stop it from being automatically unassigned in 4 days.

I've noticed a lot of people will post a comment like "I'm still working on this issue", which is good for reviewers and maintainers when looking at the issue feed. But I feel like the bot message above indicated that a comment like that will stop it from being unassigned.

**Lauryn Menard** · 2023-03-09 11:02 UTC

I wonder if there's a way to reword the message to clarify what our intention is here.

"If we don't hear back" -> "If there are no updates to the PR" ?

This is probably a bit bugged -- I would expect posting a comment to suffice.

Ok, since (as discussed here and on another thread) the expected behavior is for a comment to be sufficient to satisfy Zulipbot, would someone be up for putting up a PR to fix it?

## managing multiple PRs

*2026-01-02 to 2026-01-05 · 5 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/managing.20multiple.20PRs/with/2341302)*

**Alya Abbott** · 2026-01-02 16:40 UTC

I've noticed that I spend more time than I like managing multiple PRs for the same issue. If I remember that there's already an open PR for some issue (which I think the GitHub UI doesn't indicate at all when looking at a PR), I'll end up looking up that PR.

Them I'll usually ask the contributor of the new PR to explain the difference with old PR, as per contributor guidelines:

> Please follow the guidelines for how to [present your pull request](https://zulip.readthedocs.io/en/latest/contributing/continuing-unfinished-work.html#present-your-pull-request) when there is prior work on an issue.

**Alya Abbott** · 2026-01-02 16:42 UTC

(Sometimes I'll close the PR if the other one is undergoing review and the new one isn't clearly better.)

**Alya Abbott** · 2026-01-02 16:43 UTC

I was wondering if we could automate that reply, e.g., if there is an open PR for the same issue, and a new PR doesn't mention it, post a comment.

**Alya Abbott** · 2026-01-02 16:44 UTC

I also sometimes comment on the already-open PR to ask about the differences vs. the new PR. I think I mostly do that when the already-open PR has a "not ready" tag, so it hasn't been reviewed yet.

**Tim Abbott** · 2026-01-05 20:02 UTC

I feel like this would probably be somewhat helpful.


## unassignment timeline

*2026-01-02 to 2026-01-02 · 8 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/unassignment.20timeline/with/2341485)*

**Alya Abbott** · 2026-01-02 19:02 UTC

Zulipbot currently (unreliably due to rate limits, I believe?) unassigns issues after 14 days of inactivity:

> We noticed that you have not made any updates to this issue or linked PRs for 10 days. Please comment here if you are still actively working on it. Otherwise, we'd appreciate a quick `@zulipbot abandon` comment so that someone else can claim this issue and continue from where you left off. If we don …

I wonder if that's too much? I'm especially thinking about issues that were claimed without a PR ever being put up. I wonder if those should get a message after 5 days, and be unassigned after 7, or something like that?

**Tim Abbott** · 2026-01-02 19:44 UTC

I think the guardrail to manage is that it's very reasonable for an experienced contributor working on a larger project to spend a couple weeks before getting a PR open.

**Tim Abbott** · 2026-01-02 19:46 UTC

But for someone new to the project and open source, I could see being more aggressive. I think the risk there is mainly that if someone gets busy with school for a week, it may lead to other folks claiming the issue and then multiple PRs being generated. 

A possibly good compromise would be for the "good first issue" label to effect the timer.

**Alya Abbott** · 2026-01-02 19:49 UTC

I don't think we use that label enough for it to be worth it.

**Alya Abbott** · 2026-01-02 19:49 UTC

You can reset the timer by just commenting that you're still working on it, right?

**Alya Abbott** · 2026-01-02 19:50 UTC

You're also expected to research the issue before you claim it, so it's not 2 weeks starting totally from scratch.

**Alya Abbott** · 2026-01-02 19:51 UTC

Anyway, I don't understand why #36877 didn't get this Zulipbot warning. (And I see this kind of thing with some frequency, this is just one that I just came across.) Is it a rate limit issue, or something else?

**Tim Abbott** · 2026-01-02 20:01 UTC

Good question. Very likely rate-limiting, since that sort of batch operation is very likely to involve a big loop of API queries.

## Zulipbot needsReviewWarning and updateWarning

*2026-01-05 to 2026-01-21 · 5 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/Zulipbot.20needsReviewWarning.20and.20updateWarning/with/2343973)*

**Alya Abbott** · 2026-01-05 19:40 UTC

Anyone up for investigating why Zulipbot's needsReviewWarning and updateWarning never seem to get triggered?

**Lauryn Menard** · 2026-01-20 12:00 UTC

Pinging this conversation as there was no response to @Alya Abbott's message. Should we file an issue so this is tracked outside of this thread?

**Tim Abbott** · 2026-01-21 19:33 UTC

@Prakhar Pratyush should investigate, since he's doing some feature work on it. At least assuming @Anders Kaseorg doesn't know off the top of his head.

**Notification Bot** · 2026-01-21 19:34 UTC

This topic was moved here from #**issues>Zulipbot needsReviewWarning and updateWarning** by @Tim Abbott.

**Anders Kaseorg** · 2026-01-21 21:01 UTC

There’s nothing in the code that refers to `needsReviewWarning`; it was removed in https://github.com/zulip/zulipbot/commit/e66845e394178d6060d866e6be0a46b2c4878c78.

`updateWarning` is used based on the [`reviewed` label](https://github.com/zulip/zulip/pulls?q=label%3Areviewed), which we seem to have deleted.


## improve contributing.md

*2026-01-13 to 2026-01-13 · 8 messages · [open in Zulip](https://chat.zulip.org/#narrow/channel/105-zulipbot/topic/improve.20contributing.2Emd/with/2351765)*

**Prakhar Pratyush** · 2026-01-13 14:33 UTC

I managed to setup things locally and tested in https://github.com/prakhar1144/zulip/issues/3

**Prakhar Pratyush** · 2026-01-13 14:33 UTC

Starting this thread to note down things which we should improve (while its fresh in my head)

**Prakhar Pratyush** · 2026-01-13 14:42 UTC

[contributing.md](https://github.com/zulip/zulipbot/blob/main/.github/CONTRIBUTING.md) links to [installation wiki](https://github.com/zulip/zulipbot/wiki/Installation) page as the first step. Improvements to make there:
* Update supported `Node.js` version
* `pnpm` replacing `yarn`
* The current github web UI doesn't offer a way to change collaborator permission level to "Admin" - and it worked even without doing this.
> invited **@zulipbot**'s user account to [collaborate](https://help.github.com/articles/inviting-collaborators-to-a-personal-repository/) on your repository, changing its permission level in the repository to **Admin**.

**Prakhar Pratyush** · 2026-01-13 14:45 UTC

There's no need to follow the steps listed in the [server subsection](https://github.com/zulip/zulipbot/wiki/Installation#server) and beyond for setting up dev environment.

We use "Github App" instead of "adding a repository webhook".

**Prakhar Pratyush** · 2026-01-13 14:50 UTC

Unrelated to dev env but [Heroku ended their free plan in 2022](https://help.heroku.com/RSBRUH58/removal-of-heroku-free-product-plans-faq), so we can remove it from recommendation.

**Prakhar Pratyush** · 2026-01-13 15:07 UTC

Extend a bit on configuring "Github App":
* If creating the App using the other Github account they created for zulipbot - they need to mark it public and install it on their test repo on github.

**Prakhar Pratyush** · 2026-01-13 15:07 UTC

Putting the credentials in `/config/secrets.json` results in runtime error currently. Setting them as Node environment variables works.

**Tim Abbott** · 2026-01-13 20:09 UTC

Cool. I suggest just putting up a PR making some quick changes; realistically this repo is just used by us, so we don't need a lot of polish on the docs, just it to be clear to us and fix the errors.

**Alya Abbott** · 2026-02-28 07:20 UTC
